Imprisonment of Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva

[Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva "Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva"), the 35th and 39th [president of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Brazil "President of Brazil"), was imprisoned at the [ABC Metalworkers' Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_Metalworkers'_Union "ABC Metalworkers' Union") in [São Bernardo do Campo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Bernardo_do_Campo "São Bernardo do Campo"), in [Greater São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_S%C3%A3o_Paulo "Greater São Paulo") following his surrender to the [Federal Police of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Police_of_Brazil "Federal Police of Brazil") (PF) on 7 April 2018 until the [Supreme Federal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Federal_Court "Supreme Federal Court") (STF) overturned the arrest 8 November 2019.[&#8202;](#ref-back-1-0)[\[1\]](#refnote-1)[&#8202;](#ref-back-2-0)[\[2\]](#refnote-2)[&#8202;](#ref-back-3-0)[\[3\]](#refnote-3)[&#8202;](#ref-back-4-0)[\[4\]](#refnote-4)[&#8202;](#ref-back-5-0)[\[5\]](#refnote-5)

On 8 March 2021, Justice [Edson Fachin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edson_Fachin "Edson Fachin") overturned Lula's convictions related to [Operation Car Wash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Car_Wash "Operation Car Wash"), ruling that the [Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Regional_Court_of_the_4th_Region?action=edit&redlink=1 "Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribunal%20Regional%20Federal%20da%204.%C2%AA%20Regi%C3%A3o "pt:Tribunal Regional Federal da 4.ª Região")\] had tried the case outside its jurisdiction.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:28-0)[\[6\]](#refnote-:28) More than a year later, on 28 April 2022, the [United Nations Human Rights Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee "United Nations Human Rights Committee") published the conclusion of an analysis in which it pointed out that [Sergio Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro "Sergio Moro") had been biased in the trial of the cases against Lula.[&#8202;](#ref-back-7-0)[\[7\]](#refnote-7)[&#8202;](#ref-back-8-0)[\[8\]](#refnote-8)[&#8202;](#ref-back-9-0)[\[9\]](#refnote-9)

## Chronology

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: Chronology")\]

On 12 July 2017, [Sergio Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro "Sergio Moro"), then a federal judge of [first instance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_instance "First instance")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primeira%20inst%C3%A2ncia "pt:Primeira instância")\], sentenced [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva "Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva") to nine years and six months in prison for [passive corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passive_corruption "Passive corruption") and [money laundering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Money_laundering "Money laundering") in a criminal case involving a triplex [apartment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartment "Apartment") in [Guarujá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaruj%C3%A1 "Guarujá"), on the [coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_coastline?action=edit&redlink=1 "São Paulo coastline (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Litoral%20de%20S%C3%A3o%20Paulo "pt:Litoral de São Paulo")\] of the state of [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo "São Paulo").[&#8202;](#ref-back-10-0)[\[10\]](#refnote-10)[&#8202;](#ref-back-11-0)[\[11\]](#refnote-11) In the [second instance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_instance "Second instance"), the sentence was increased to 12 years and one month, after a unanimous vote.[&#8202;](#ref-back-12-0)[\[12\]](#refnote-12) Then, on April 5, Moro issued the arrest warrant.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:7-0)[\[13\]](#refnote-:7) In April 2019, in a unanimous decision, the 5th Panel of the [Superior Court of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Court_of_Justice_(Brazil) "Superior Court of Justice (Brazil)") (STJ) upheld Lula's conviction and reduced the sentence for passive corruption and money laundering from 12 years and one month to eight years and ten months in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-14-0)[\[14\]](#refnote-14) The triplex case was investigated as part of [Operation Car Wash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Car_Wash "Operation Car Wash").[&#8202;](#ref-back-15-0)[\[15\]](#refnote-15) Lula served his sentence in [Curitiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curitiba "Curitiba"), capital of [Paraná](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paran%C3%A1_(state) "Paraná (state)"), in the building of the Regional Superintendency of the Federal Police in Paraná.[&#8202;](#ref-back-16-0)[\[16\]](#refnote-16) In August 2018, the [United Nations Human Rights Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee "United Nations Human Rights Committee") requested that Brazil guarantee Lula the right to exercise his political rights while in prison, including access to members of his party and the media and participation in the 2018 presidential election in Brazil.[&#8202;](#ref-back-17-0)[\[17\]](#refnote-17)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:25-0)[\[18\]](#refnote-:25) The [Brazilian Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Brazil) "Ministry of Foreign Affairs (Brazil)") questioned the Committee and referred the decision to the [Judiciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary_of_Brazil "Judiciary of Brazil").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:26-0)[\[19\]](#refnote-:26)

On 8 March 2021, Justice [Edson Fachin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edson_Fachin "Edson Fachin") overturned Lula's convictions related to Operation Car Wash, ruling that the [Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Regional_Court_of_the_4th_Region?action=edit&redlink=1 "Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribunal%20Regional%20Federal%20da%204.%C2%AA%20Regi%C3%A3o "pt:Tribunal Regional Federal da 4.ª Região")\] had tried the case outside its jurisdiction, based on a previous decision by the Second Panel of the [Supreme Federal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Federal_Court "Supreme Federal Court") (STF) in the case of [Transpetro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transpetro "Transpetro"), a Brazilian company linked to [Petrobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrobras "Petrobras"), responsible for fuel [transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport "Transport") and [logistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logistics "Logistics"), which operates in the import and export of oil and oil products, gas, and ethanol.[&#8202;](#ref-back-20-0)[\[20\]](#refnote-20) It was determined that the case should be tried in [Brasília](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bras%C3%ADlia "Brasília"). As a result, Lula regained his [political rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_and_political_rights "Civil and political rights") and the [legal proceedings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_proceeding "Legal proceeding") against him were transferred to the [Federal Regional Court of the 1st Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Regional_Court_of_the_1st_Region?action=edit&redlink=1 "Federal Regional Court of the 1st Region (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribunal%20Regional%20Federal%20da%201.%C2%AA%20Regi%C3%A3o "pt:Tribunal Regional Federal da 1.ª Região")\].[&#8202;](#ref-back-:33-0)[\[21\]](#refnote-:33)[&#8202;](#ref-back-22-0)[\[22\]](#refnote-22) Subsequently, the Guarujá triplex case, which led to Lula's imprisonment, was dismissed by the court.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-0)[\[23\]](#refnote-:2)

On 28 April 2022, the [United Nations Human Rights Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee "United Nations Human Rights Committee") published the conclusion of an analysis in which it pointed out that Sergio Moro had been biased in the trial of the cases against Lula.[&#8202;](#ref-back-24-0)[\[24\]](#refnote-24) According to the Committee's decision, Lula's political rights were violated in 2018 after he was prevented from participating in that [year's presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Brazilian_general_election "2018 Brazilian general election"). The Committee determined that Brazil must present the measures adopted to repair the damage caused to the former president within 180 days and prevent other people from being targeted by similar proceedings in the future.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:22-0)[\[25\]](#refnote-:22)[&#8202;](#ref-back-26-0)[\[26\]](#refnote-26) Lula was the first former president of Brazil to be imprisoned for a conviction for a common crime.[&#8202;](#ref-back-27-0)[\[27\]](#refnote-27) Before him, six other former presidents of the country were imprisoned: [Hermes da Fonseca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermes_da_Fonseca "Hermes da Fonseca"), [Washington Luís](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Lu%C3%ADs "Washington Luís"), [Artur Bernardes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artur_Bernardes "Artur Bernardes"), [Café Filho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caf%C3%A9_Filho "Café Filho"), [Jânio Quadros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%A2nio_Quadros "Jânio Quadros"), and [Juscelino Kubitschek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juscelino_Kubitschek "Juscelino Kubitschek"), all for explicit political reasons.[&#8202;](#ref-back-28-0)[\[28\]](#refnote-28)[&#8202;](#ref-back-29-0)[\[29\]](#refnote-29)

## Overview

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Overview")\]

### List of criminal cases involving Lula

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: List of criminal cases involving Lula")\]

The triplex case was one of several cases in which Lula was involved. Lula was a defendant in ten criminal cases in total, including some in which he has already been acquitted or convicted. Some of these criminal cases are part of Operation Car Wash.[&#8202;](#ref-back-30-0)[\[30\]](#refnote-30)

| # | Judge | Reason / Charges | Date | Status / Outcome |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Ricardo Leite | The judge accepted the complaint filed by the Federal Public Prosecutor's Office (MPF) and charged Lula with obstruction of justice. Also charged were former senator [Delcídio do Amaral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delc%C3%ADdio_do_Amaral "Delcídio do Amaral") (independent-MS), Delcídio's former chief of staff Diogo Ferreira, banker [André Esteves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Esteves "André Esteves"), lawyer Édson Ribeiro, cattle rancher [José Carlos Bumlai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Carlos_Bumlai?action=edit&redlink=1 "José Carlos Bumlai (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9%20Carlos%20Bumlai "pt:José Carlos Bumlai")\], and his son, Maurício Bumlai. The former president was accused of trying to prevent former Petrobras director [Nestor Cerveró](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nestor_Cerver%C3%B3?action=edit&redlink=1 "Nestor Cerveró (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nestor%20Cerver%C3%B3 "pt:Nestor Cerveró")\] from signing a plea bargain agreement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-31-0)[\[31\]](#refnote-31) | 29 July 2016 | Acquitted[&#8202;](#ref-back-32-0)[\[32\]](#refnote-32) |
| 2 | [Sergio Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro "Sergio Moro") | Accepted MPF complaint, making Lula a defendant for [passive corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passive_corruption "Passive corruption") and [money laundering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Money_laundering "Money laundering").[&#8202;](#ref-back-33-0)[\[33\]](#refnote-33)[&#8202;](#ref-back-34-0)[\[34\]](#refnote-34) Lula allegedly received over R$3 million in bribes from construction company [OAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grupo_Metha?action=edit&redlink=1 "Grupo Metha (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grupo%20Metha "pt:Grupo Metha")\] in the form of renovations to a triplex apartment in [Guarujá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaruj%C3%A1 "Guarujá"), plus storage of personal goods.[&#8202;](#ref-back-35-0)[\[35\]](#refnote-35) | 20 September 2016 | Convicted in three instances (initially 9 years and 6 months by Moro), but convictions annulled by the Supreme Federal Court (STF) due to lack of jurisdiction in Curitiba and partiality of Moro; case later archived in Brasília.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-1)[\[23\]](#refnote-:2)[&#8202;](#ref-back-36-0)[\[36\]](#refnote-36) |
| 3 | Vallisney de Souza Oliveira | He fully accepted the Federal Prosecutor's Office's complaint against former President Lula for the crimes of [influence peddling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Influence_peddling "Influence peddling"), [criminal organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_organization "Criminal organization"), money laundering, and passive corruption in [Operation Janus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Janus "Operation Janus")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera%C3%A7%C3%A3o%20Janus "pt:Operação Janus")\], which investigates suspicious business deals in [Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola "Angola") involving money from the [Brazilian Development Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Development_Bank "Brazilian Development Bank") (BNDES) funds.[&#8202;](#ref-back-37-0)[\[37\]](#refnote-37)[&#8202;](#ref-back-38-0)[\[38\]](#refnote-38) | 13 October 2016 | Acquitted on some charges; action later terminated/closed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-39-0)[\[39\]](#refnote-39)[&#8202;](#ref-back-40-0)[\[40\]](#refnote-40)[&#8202;](#ref-back-41-0)[\[41\]](#refnote-41) |
| 4 | Vallisney de Souza Oliveira | Accepted MPF complaint in [Zelotes operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zelotes_operation?action=edit&redlink=1 "Zelotes operation (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera%C3%A7%C3%A3o%20Zelotes "pt:Operação Zelotes")\], making Lula a defendant for influence peddling, money laundering, and criminal organization related to the purchase of 36 Swedish Gripen fighters and extension of tax incentives via Provisional Measure 627.[&#8202;](#ref-back-42-0)[\[42\]](#refnote-42) | 17 December 2016 | Criminal action suspended.[&#8202;](#ref-back-43-0)[\[43\]](#refnote-43) |
| 5 | Sergio Moro | A new complaint filed by the Car Wash task force against former President Lula has been accepted. With this decision, Lula becomes a defendant for the crimes of passive corruption and money laundering. He was accused of receiving kickbacks from Odebrecht for the purchase of an apartment in [São Bernardo do Campo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Bernardo_do_Campo "São Bernardo do Campo") and another property in São Paulo. According to the indictment, this location was intended for the [Lula Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lula_Institute?action=edit&redlink=1 "Lula Institute (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instituto%20Lula "pt:Instituto Lula")\].[&#8202;](#ref-back-44-0)[\[44\]](#refnote-44) | 19 December 2016 | Criminal action suspended.[&#8202;](#ref-back-45-0)[\[45\]](#refnote-45) |
| 6 | Sergio Moro | He accepted another complaint against Lula, related to the case of a ranch in Atibaia, a city located in the interior of the state of São Paulo. Lula was accused of corruption and money laundering in the ranch case. According to the indictment, the former president benefited from R$1.02 million in renovations to the property, which was frequented by Lula and his family. The renovations were allegedly paid for by the contractors Odebrecht and OAS.[&#8202;](#ref-back-46-0)[\[46\]](#refnote-46) | 1 August 2017 | Convicted in two instances, but the conviction was overturned and the complaint dismissed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-47-0)[\[47\]](#refnote-47)[&#8202;](#ref-back-48-0)[\[48\]](#refnote-48) |
| 7 | Vallisney de Souza Oliveira | Accused of selling provisional measures granting tax incentives to automakers during his presidency. The judge indicted him for passive corruption.[&#8202;](#ref-back-49-0)[\[49\]](#refnote-49) | 1 September 2017 | Acquitted.[&#8202;](#ref-back-50-0)[\[50\]](#refnote-50) |
| 8 | Vallisney de Souza Oliveira | He accepted a complaint filed by the Attorney General's Office in 2017 against Lula, initiating criminal proceedings and making him a defendant for the eighth time, in a case that became known as “Quadrilhão do PT” (PT Gang). Former President [Dilma Rousseff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilma_Rousseff "Dilma Rousseff") also became a defendant, among others.[&#8202;](#ref-back-51-0)[\[51\]](#refnote-51) | 23 November 2018 | Acquitted.[&#8202;](#ref-back-52-0)[\[52\]](#refnote-52) |
| 9 | [Federal Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Justice?action=edit&redlink=1 "Federal Justice (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justi%C3%A7a%20Federal "pt:Justiça Federal")\] | Lula was accused of receiving one million reais, through a donation to the Lula Institute, to mediate discussions between the government of [Equatorial Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equatorial_Guinea "Equatorial Guinea") and the Brazilian group ARG, with the aim of establishing the company in the country.[&#8202;](#ref-back-53-0)[\[53\]](#refnote-53)[&#8202;](#ref-back-54-0)[\[54\]](#refnote-54) | 14 December 2018 | Criminal proceedings closed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-55-0)[\[55\]](#refnote-55) |
| 10 | Federal Court of São Paulo | Lula was accused of international influence peddling on behalf of the construction company OAS. The accusation stated that Lula had given a speech in [Costa Rica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costa_Rica "Costa Rica") in 2011 in order to influence the country's leaders to do business with OAS.\[ _[clarification needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")_\][&#8202;](#ref-back-56-0)[\[56\]](#refnote-56) | 13 September 2021 | Case archived.[&#8202;](#ref-back-57-0)[\[57\]](#refnote-57)[&#8202;](#ref-back-58-0)[\[58\]](#refnote-58) |
| _Updated in January 2026. Most of the Car Wash cases involving Lula were overturned by the Supreme Court in 2021 due to jurisdictional issues and judicial bias; subsequent cases in Brasília or elsewhere resulted in acquittals, dismissals, or suspensions due to lack of evidence or procedural reasons._ |  |  |  |  |

## Background

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Background")\]

In March 2010, [Rio de Janeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_de_Janeiro "Rio de Janeiro") newspaper _[O Globo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Globo "O Globo")_ published a report about an apartment in the city of [Guarujá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaruj%C3%A1 "Guarujá"), in the state of [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_(state) "São Paulo (state)"), that allegedly belonged to then-President Lula.[&#8202;](#ref-back-guaruja-0)[\[59\]](#refnote-guaruja) There were no suspicions of illegality at the time; the report focused on a multiple year delay in constructing the apartment complex, and its construction being taken over by [Construtora OAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construtora_OAS?action=edit&redlink=1 "Construtora OAS (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construtora%20OAS "pt:Construtora OAS")\].[&#8202;](#ref-back-guaruja-1)[\[59\]](#refnote-guaruja)

### Operation Car Wash

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Operation Car Wash")\]

Main article: [Operation Car Wash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Car_Wash "Operation Car Wash")

In March 2014, an anti-corruption probe nicknamed Operation Car Wash (_Operação Lava Jato_) began; it would become the largest anti-corruption operation in the history of Brazil, revealing corruption and illegal payments in [procurements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_procurement "Government procurement") of [public works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_works "Public works"), many related to [Petrobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrobras "Petrobras").[&#8202;](#ref-back-cnn-car-wash-0)[\[60\]](#refnote-cnn-car-wash) Several companies – [Odebrecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odebrecht "Odebrecht"), [Camargo Corrêa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camargo_Corr%C3%AAa "Camargo Corrêa"), Construtora OAS, among others – were accused of [bribing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kickback_(finance) "Kickback (finance)") Petrobras employees and higher-ups to only choose their companies for Petrobras procurements; the companies would then decide, among themselves, on an overpriced invoice[&#8202;](#ref-back-61-0)[\[a\]](#refnote-61) and pocket the difference.[&#8202;](#ref-back-cnn-car-wash-1)[\[60\]](#refnote-cnn-car-wash) Those bribes were initially made in cash, but would later take the form of [offshore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Offshore_financial_centre "Offshore financial centre") transfers or the paying off of properties.[&#8202;](#ref-back-cnn-car-wash-2)[\[60\]](#refnote-cnn-car-wash)

The investigation expanded through [plea deals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plea_deal "Plea deal"), such that over 200 arrests and 1 thousand [search and seizure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Search_and_seizure "Search and seizure") operations were made, recovering over [R$](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_real "Brazilian real") 4 billion in total (over [US$](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_dollar "United States dollar") 1 billion).[&#8202;](#ref-back-cnn-car-wash-3)[\[60\]](#refnote-cnn-car-wash)

### Societal context

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Societal context")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Manifesta%C3%A7%C3%A3o_em_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_contra_corrup%C3%A7%C3%A3o_e_o_governo_Dilma_em_13_de_mar%C3%A7o_de_2016_%283%29.jpg/250px-Manifesta%C3%A7%C3%A3o_em_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_contra_corrup%C3%A7%C3%A3o_e_o_governo_Dilma_em_13_de_mar%C3%A7o_de_2016_%283%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Manifesta%C3%A7%C3%A3o_em_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_contra_corrup%C3%A7%C3%A3o_e_o_governo_Dilma_em_13_de_mar%C3%A7o_de_2016_(3).jpg) March 2016 protests in support of [Sergio Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro "Sergio Moro"), calling for Lula's arrest, the ousting of then-president Dilma Rousseff and the [Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers'_Party_(Brazil) "Workers' Party (Brazil)") from power, and lower taxes

Operation Car Wash coincided with an explosive growth in what was termed "_antipetismo_" (an "anti-Workers' Party" sentiment) around 2014.[&#8202;](#ref-back-62-0)[\[61\]](#refnote-62) This was motivated in part by a dissatisfaction with the economy: during the latest few years of a Worker's Party government, the [Brazilian real](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_real "Brazilian real") had been losing strength in the USD exchange. In March 2015, for example, it hit a decade-long high, at USD$1 = R$3.16.[&#8202;](#ref-back-63-0)[\[62\]](#refnote-63)

Protests broke out throughout the country calling for then- [president Dilma Rousseff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Dilma_Rousseff "Presidency of Dilma Rousseff")'s removal from office, be it through an [impeachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment "Impeachment") or military intervention, as part of a movement called "_Vem pra Rua_" (lit.'Come to the Streets').[&#8202;](#ref-back-telles2016-0)[\[63\]](#refnote-telles2016) An April 2015 poll of protestors put over 90%, a majority of which were middle- and upper-class [White Brazilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Brazilians "White Brazilians"), believing the Workers' Party did a "great harm" to Brazil; and 37% believing minorities such as [Afro-Brazilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afro-Brazilians "Afro-Brazilians"), women and homosexuals had "too many rights" in Brazil.[&#8202;](#ref-back-telles2016-1)[\[63\]](#refnote-telles2016)

### Lula involvement

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Lula involvement")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Lula_em_entrevista_no_Diret%C3%B3rio_Nacional_do_PT_em_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_1.jpg/250px-Lula_em_entrevista_no_Diret%C3%B3rio_Nacional_do_PT_em_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lula_em_entrevista_no_Diret%C3%B3rio_Nacional_do_PT_em_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_1.jpg) Lula speaking at press conference after returning from his coerced summons

In March 2016, [Lula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva "Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva") was the target of a [coerced summons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condu%C3%A7%C3%A3o_coercitiva "Condução coercitiva") (_condução coercitiva_)[&#8202;](#ref-back-65-0)[\[b\]](#refnote-65) to testify on suspicions of receiving bribes by Construtora OAS and Odebrecht in the form of renovations of an apartment in [Guarujá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaruj%C3%A1 "Guarujá") and a country house[&#8202;](#ref-back-66-0)[\[c\]](#refnote-66) in [Atibaia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atibaia "Atibaia"), both in the state of [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo_(state) "São Paulo (state)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-cnn-car-wash-4)[\[60\]](#refnote-cnn-car-wash) Lula was also under investigation for money received as donations and payments for lectures.[&#8202;](#ref-back-lula-coercitiva-0)[\[64\]](#refnote-lula-coercitiva)

At the time, Lula criticized the coercion aspect of the summons, stating he wouldn't have refused to testify if asked.[&#8202;](#ref-back-lula-coercitiva-1)[\[64\]](#refnote-lula-coercitiva) He also stated that were [Federal Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Police_of_Brazil "Federal Police of Brazil") to find a single [real](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_real "Brazilian real") of his came from [embezzlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embezzlement "Embezzlement"), he didn't deserve to be a part of the [Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers'_Party_(Brazil) "Workers' Party (Brazil)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-lula-coercitiva-2)[\[64\]](#refnote-lula-coercitiva) Lula further criticized that his lawyers were not informed of "anything", while some in the [news media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_media "News media") had prior knowledge of the intricacies of the investigation: "it's regretful that a portion of the Brazilian Judiciary is working in association with the press".[&#8202;](#ref-back-lula-coercitiva-3)[\[64\]](#refnote-lula-coercitiva)

Lula also justified what was seen as suspiciously high prices for his lectures: "People wanted Lula to speak on the things he did in Brazil. The miracle he did to approve [Prouni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Programa_Universidade_para_Todos "Programa Universidade para Todos"), [Fies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundo_de_Financiamento_ao_Estudante_do_Ensino_Superior "Fundo de Financiamento ao Estudante do Ensino Superior"), to bring energy to 15 million of the poor in this country. That's why I became the most expensive speaker in the world, alongside [Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton"). \[...\] I don't have a [mongrel complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongrel_complex "Mongrel complex"). I know what I did for the country".[&#8202;](#ref-back-lula-coercitiva-4)[\[64\]](#refnote-lula-coercitiva)

### Accusation

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Accusation")\]

A few days after his summons, São Paulo state's [Public Prosecutor's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Prosecutor's_Office_(Brazil) "Public Prosecutor's Office (Brazil)") (MP-SP) formally accused Lula and his wife, [Marisa Letícia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marisa_Let%C3%ADcia_Lula_da_Silva "Marisa Letícia Lula da Silva"), of [money laundering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Money_laundering "Money laundering") and concealment of assets.[&#8202;](#ref-back-elpais-chronology-0)[\[65\]](#refnote-elpais-chronology) According to the prosecutors, Lula was the _de facto_ owner of the Guarujá apartment (even if it was legally still property of the Construtora OAS company), which they argued constituted money laundering.[&#8202;](#ref-back-guaruja-2016-accusation-0)[\[66\]](#refnote-guaruja-2016-accusation)

Crucially, the São Paulo state courts sent the accusation papers to the federal courts in [Curitiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curitiba "Curitiba"), in the state of [Paraná](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paran%C3%A1_(state) "Paraná (state)"), to be ruled on by [Sergio Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro "Sergio Moro").[&#8202;](#ref-back-70-0)[\[67\]](#refnote-70) At the time, there was already an open [Supreme Federal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Federal_Court "Supreme Federal Court") case asking its deliberation on whether the prosecution would fall under the federal or São Paulo state jurisdictions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-guaruja-2016-accusation-1)[\[66\]](#refnote-guaruja-2016-accusation)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Slide_de_coletiva_de_imprensa_apresentando_den%C3%BAncia_a_Lula.jpg/250px-Slide_de_coletiva_de_imprensa_apresentando_den%C3%BAncia_a_Lula.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Slide_de_coletiva_de_imprensa_apresentando_den%C3%BAncia_a_Lula.jpg) Federal prosecution presented a [slide show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slide_show "Slide show") to explain the September 2016 accusations against Lula; it quickly became [viral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viral_phenomenon "Viral phenomenon") online for its grammatical errors and absurdity.[&#8202;](#ref-back-71-0)[\[68\]](#refnote-71)[&#8202;](#ref-back-72-0)[\[69\]](#refnote-72)[&#8202;](#ref-back-73-0)[\[70\]](#refnote-73)

Adding to the complaints, in September 2016, the Federal Public Prosecutor's Office accused Lula and seven other people of corruption and money laundering.[&#8202;](#ref-back-federal-accusation-0)[\[71\]](#refnote-federal-accusation) According to the federal prosecutors, Lula was the "supreme commander" of the corruption scheme revealed by Operation Car Wash involving three contracts between Construtora OAS and Petrobras; Lula had allegedly received almost R$4 million in bribes, paid not in money but through renovations of the Guarujá apartment and the storage of personal items.[&#8202;](#ref-back-federal-accusation-1)[\[71\]](#refnote-federal-accusation) A few days later, judge Sergio Moro accepted the Prosecutor's Office complaint, and Lula officially became a defendant in Operation Car Wash.[&#8202;](#ref-back-75-0)[\[72\]](#refnote-75)

After being charged, Lula gave a speech: "I respect the institutions and respect the laws. I'll testify as many times as they want. \[&#8202;](#ref-back-sep2016-speech-0)[...\] When I infringe on the law, punish me to make me an example. But when I don't, find someone else to mess with".[\[73\]](#refnote-sep2016-speech) Lula also praised the Transparency Portal and the _Lei de Acesso à Informação_ (lit.'Information Access Law'), created during his and Dilma's governments; and stated: "Prove I've been corrupt and I'll surrender by foot".[&#8202;](#ref-back-sep2016-speech-1)[\[73\]](#refnote-sep2016-speech)

By May 2017, Lula was accused of a further 10 corruption and 44 money laundering cases relating to renovations of a country house in Atibaia; though it was not owned by Lula, the prosecution argued the renovations were bribes in his benefit by the companies Odebrecht, Construtora OAS e Schahin.[&#8202;](#ref-back-77-0)[\[74\]](#refnote-77)

Lula's lawyers filed a lawsuit against prosecutor [Deltan Dallagnol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deltan_Dallagnol "Deltan Dallagnol"),\[ _[_non sequitur_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")_\] seeking compensation for moral damages for the press conference given by Dallagnol in 2016 in which he used a [Microsoft PowerPoint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft_PowerPoint "Microsoft PowerPoint") presentation to explain the charges against Lula.[&#8202;](#ref-back-78-0)[\[75\]](#refnote-78) Initially, Dallagnol was acquitted in the first and second instances, but when the case reached the [Superior Court of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Court_of_Justice_(Brazil) "Superior Court of Justice (Brazil)") (STJ), the court ordered Dallagnol to pay Lula 146,000 reais in damages.[&#8202;](#ref-back-79-0)[\[76\]](#refnote-79) The ministers of the fourth panel of the STJ considered that Deltan Dallagnol exceeded the limits of his duties as a prosecutor by using language that discredited Lula's honor and image at the press conference, in addition to using non-technical language.[&#8202;](#ref-back-80-0)[\[77\]](#refnote-80) The STJ ministers also considered that Dallagnol attributed to Lula facts that were not included in the complaint in the triplex case, as explained during the press conference.[&#8202;](#ref-back-81-0)[\[78\]](#refnote-81)[&#8202;](#ref-back-82-0)[\[79\]](#refnote-82) The Supreme Federal Court upheld the STJ's ruling, ordering Dallagnol to pay damages to Lula.[&#8202;](#ref-back-83-0)[\[80\]](#refnote-83)

### Defense

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Defense")\]

In 2016,\[ _[when?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")_\] Lula's defense team submitted a request to the [United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations "United Nations") (UN)\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] to assess possible violations in the legal proceedings involving Lula under the [International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Covenant_on_Civil_and_Political_Rights "International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights"), to which Brazil is a signatory. In May 2018, the organization's Human Rights Committee stated that it would not take a final position until 2019, but denied precautionary measures against the former president's imprisonment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-84-0)[\[81\]](#refnote-84)\[ _[needs update](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")_\]

### Testimonials

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Testimonials")\]

On 20 April 2017, former OAS president Léo Pinheiro testified before Judge Sergio Moro, saying that the triplex did indeed belong to Lula and that the apartment had never been put up for sale by OAS because it was reserved for the former president. He also said that Lula himself had approved the design of the triplex and the ranch in Atibaia, which was investigated in another case. Lula's defense claimed that Léo Pinheiro was lying in order to try to secure a plea bargain.[&#8202;](#ref-back-85-0)[\[82\]](#refnote-85) About a year earlier, the newspaper _[Folha de S.Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folha_de_S.Paulo "Folha de S.Paulo")_ reported that Léo Pinheiro had told another version of the story, in which he exonerated Lula, but prosecutors considered this version implausible.[&#8202;](#ref-back-86-0)[\[83\]](#refnote-86)

On 10 May 2017, Lula gave his testimony to Judge Sergio Moro about the triplex case. During the interrogation, which lasted almost five hours, the former president denied owning the apartment and said he never intended to purchase it.[&#8202;](#ref-back-87-0)[\[84\]](#refnote-87) Lula said he only visited the triplex once to see what it was like and did not want to buy it.[&#8202;](#ref-back-88-0)[\[85\]](#refnote-88) He also said that his wife, [Marisa Letícia Lula da Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marisa_Let%C3%ADcia_Lula_da_Silva "Marisa Letícia Lula da Silva"), was interested in the property and that she had tried to purchase the apartment without telling him about it.[&#8202;](#ref-back-89-0)[\[86\]](#refnote-89) The testimony had a major impact in Brazil and in the international press.[&#8202;](#ref-back-90-0)[\[87\]](#refnote-90) In Curitiba, where the testimony was given, protesters held demonstrations for and against the former president throughout the day.[&#8202;](#ref-back-91-0)[\[88\]](#refnote-91)[&#8202;](#ref-back-92-0)[\[89\]](#refnote-92)

## Convictions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Convictions")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Juiz-Sergio-Moro-comiss%C3%A3o-combate-corrup%C3%A7%C3%A3o.jpg/250px-Juiz-Sergio-Moro-comiss%C3%A3o-combate-corrup%C3%A7%C3%A3o.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Juiz-Sergio-Moro-comiss%C3%A3o-combate-corrup%C3%A7%C3%A3o.jpg) Sergio Moro, former judge in the Lava Jato investigation, who would later become [Minister of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Justice_and_Public_Security_(Brazil) "Ministry of Justice and Public Security (Brazil)") in the [Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro "Jair Bolsonaro") [administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Jair_Bolsonaro "Presidency of Jair Bolsonaro").

On 12 July 2017, federal judge Sergio Moro sentenced former president Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva to nine years and six months in prison in the criminal case involving the triplex apartment, for the crimes of passive corruption and money laundering.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:4-0)[\[90\]](#refnote-:4) The indictment referred to the concealment of ownership of a triplex penthouse in Guarujá, on the coast of São Paulo, received as a bribe from the construction company OAS in exchange for favors at Petrobras.[&#8202;](#ref-back-94-0)[\[91\]](#refnote-94) Three months later, Lula gave a new statement to Sergio Moro, this time in a case involving the [Lula Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lula_Institute?action=edit&redlink=1 "Lula Institute (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instituto%20Lula "pt:Instituto Lula")\].[&#8202;](#ref-back-95-0)[\[92\]](#refnote-95) During the testimony, Lula questioned Moro and said that he had not been judged impartially by him in the triplex case.[&#8202;](#ref-back-96-0)[\[93\]](#refnote-96) Moro declined to comment and simply told Lula to question the triplex case in the court of appeals.[&#8202;](#ref-back-97-0)[\[94\]](#refnote-97)

### Appeal court

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Appeal court")\]

On 24 January 2018, in a trial that lasted more than eight hours and received international attention, Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva was convicted in the second instance.[&#8202;](#ref-back-98-0)[\[95\]](#refnote-98) The sentence was unanimous among the three judges of the 8th Panel of the Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region (TRF-4), in [Porto Alegre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porto_Alegre "Porto Alegre"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-99-0)[\[96\]](#refnote-99) in favor of upholding the previous conviction, which was 9 years and 6 months in prison for passive corruption and money laundering, and further extending the former president's prison sentence for passive corruption and money laundering in the case of the triplex apartment in Guarujá.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:3-0)[\[97\]](#refnote-:3) The judges decided to extend the sentence to 12 years and 1 month in prison, to be served in a closed regime.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:3-1)[\[97\]](#refnote-:3) The conclusions were that Lula received a [bribe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bribery "Bribery") from a corruption scheme at Petrobras and that this bribe was delivered in the form of an apartment. The sentence would begin after all possible appeals within the TRF-4 itself had been exhausted. Even though he was convicted, he did not immediately lose the right to run in the 2018 elections, a decision that would be up to the [Superior Electoral Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Electoral_Court "Superior Electoral Court") (TSE).[&#8202;](#ref-back-:3-2)[\[97\]](#refnote-:3)[&#8202;](#ref-back-101-0)[\[98\]](#refnote-101) Lula's defense team and politicians close to him questioned the speed with which his case was processed in the TRF-4. The then president of the court, Thompson Flores, responded that speed is “a common feature of this court” and that “the time taken to process the appeal does not violate the principle of equality.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-102-0)[\[99\]](#refnote-102)[&#8202;](#ref-back-103-0)[\[100\]](#refnote-103)[&#8202;](#ref-back-104-0)[\[101\]](#refnote-104)

#### Evidences

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: Evidences")\]

According to the judge in charge of the case, João Pedro Gebran Neto, the evidence of Lula's involvement in passive corruption and money laundering in the Guarujá triplex case includes:[&#8202;](#ref-back-:5-0)[\[102\]](#refnote-:5)

- Testimony from Leo Pinheiro, former president of OAS, and Agenor Franklin Magalhães Medeiros, director of OAS's Oil and Gas division, both defendants in the case;
- Private text messages from Leo Pinheiro referring to a project headed by a "boss" and a "madam", who were, respectively, Lula and his wife Marisa Letícia;
- Testimony from OAS employees and contractors hired to renovate the property, as well as from an employee of the Solaris building, regarding the construction work and the couple's visits to the triplex;
- Documents with erasures regarding a property in Guarujá, found at Lula's home and at Bancoop;
- Testimony from whistleblowers in the Lava Jato investigation, such as [Alberto Youssef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Youssef "Alberto Youssef"), Nestor Cerveró, and [Paulo Roberto Costa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo_Roberto_Costa?action=edit&redlink=1 "Paulo Roberto Costa (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulo%20Roberto%20Costa "pt:Paulo Roberto Costa")\], consistent with Leo Pinheiro's statements about corruption at Petrobras;

According to Gebran, the evidence that the apartment "was intended for Lula as a benefit" was "beyond reasonable doubt" and Lula was aware of what was happening, being a "guarantor of a larger scheme" and "acting behind the scenes", influencing Petrobras appointments.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:5-1)[\[102\]](#refnote-:5) Gebran also rejected Lula's defense claim that there was no official act by the former president, citing votes from the [Mensalão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mensal%C3%A3o_scandal "Mensalão scandal") trial, and rejected the defense's appeal that the 13th Federal Court, presided over by Judge Sergio Moro, lacked jurisdiction to hear the case.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:5-2)[\[102\]](#refnote-:5)

However, some analysts disagreed with the evidence presented, saying that it was more indicative of a possible crime than concrete proof, which would weaken the conviction.[&#8202;](#ref-back-106-0)[\[103\]](#refnote-106)[&#8202;](#ref-back-107-0)[\[104\]](#refnote-107)[&#8202;](#ref-back-108-0)[\[105\]](#refnote-108) The former [Minister of National Integration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Integration_and_Regional_Development "Ministry of Integration and Regional Development") and 2018 presidential candidate, [Ciro Gomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciro_Gomes "Ciro Gomes"), took the same line and said that Lula was convicted based on "circumstantial evidence".[&#8202;](#ref-back-:30-0)[\[106\]](#refnote-:30) Other analysts argued that the Guarujá triplex was indeed intended for Lula, but that it was not clear in the ruling what act of corruption he had committed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-110-0)[\[107\]](#refnote-110) Journalist [Reinaldo Azevedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinaldo_Azevedo "Reinaldo Azevedo"), former columnist for _[Veja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veja_(magazine) "Veja (magazine)")_ magazine and vocal critic of the [Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers'_Party_(Brazil) "Workers' Party (Brazil)") (PT), defended Lula against Moro's accusations, stating that a reading of the case reveals the absence of such solid evidence, with the Federal Public Prosecutor's Office's complaint being ignored in crucial parts and the court decision resorting to broad and politicized interpretations, which would constitute a violation of the principle of presumption of innocence and due process of law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:4-1)[\[90\]](#refnote-:4)[&#8202;](#ref-back-111-0)[\[108\]](#refnote-111)[&#8202;](#ref-back-112-0)[\[109\]](#refnote-112)

#### Preventive _habeas corpus_

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Preventive habeas corpus")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Manifesta%C3%A7%C3%A3o_%2840064590425%29.jpg/250px-Manifesta%C3%A7%C3%A3o_%2840064590425%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Manifesta%C3%A7%C3%A3o_(40064590425).jpg) Demonstration on March 22 in front of the Supreme Court for _habeas corpus_.

After Lula's conviction in the second instance, the former president's defense team filed preventive _[habeas corpus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habeas_corpus "Habeas corpus")_ petitions with the Superior Court of Justice and the Supreme Court in an attempt to prevent his imprisonment.

On 6 March 2018, the Superior Court of Justice unanimously rejected Lula's habeas corpus petition by a vote of 5 to 0.[&#8202;](#ref-back-113-0)[\[110\]](#refnote-113)

|  |
| --- |
| **Eduardo Villas Boas**<br>(@Gen\_VillasBoas)<br> tweeted: |

> Nessa situação que vive o Brasil, resta perguntar às instituições e ao povo quem realmente está pensando no bem do País e das gerações futuras e quem está preocupado apenas com interesses pessoais?

* * *

Translation:

> In the current situation in Brazil, we must ask the institutions and the people: who is truly thinking about the good of the country and future generations, and who is only concerned with personal interests?

3 April 2018[&#8202;](#ref-back-boasNessa-0)[\[111\]](#refnote-boasNessa)

|  |
| --- |
| **Eduardo Villas Boas**<br>(@Gen\_VillasBoas)<br> tweeted: |

> Asseguro à Nação que o Exército Brasileiro julga compartilhar o anseio de todos os cidadãos de bem de repúdio à impunidade e de respeito à Constituição, à paz social e à Democracia, bem como se mantém atento às suas missões institucionais.

* * *

Translation:

> I assure the nation that the Brazilian Army shares the desire of all law-abiding citizens to reject impunity and respect the Constitution, social peace, and democracy, while remaining attentive to its institutional missions.

3 April 2018[&#8202;](#ref-back-boasAsseguro-0)[\[112\]](#refnote-boasAsseguro)

On 22 March 2018, the STF accepted a request from the defense and granted the former president safe conduct—preventing Lula's arrest until the final ruling on the habeas corpus petition by the court itself on April 4.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:6-0)[\[113\]](#refnote-:6) According to the STF's understanding of imprisonment in the second instance, the Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region could issue an arrest warrant against the former president after the last appeal was heard in court on March 26, 2018.[&#8202;](#ref-back-117-0)[\[114\]](#refnote-117)[&#8202;](#ref-back-118-0)[\[115\]](#refnote-118) However, the “Lula principle,” as it became known, prevented the TRF-4 from enforcing the prison sentence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:6-1)[\[113\]](#refnote-:6)[&#8202;](#ref-back-119-0)[\[116\]](#refnote-119) The Supreme Court's decision was criticized by federal prosecutors and prosecutors from the Car Wash task force.[&#8202;](#ref-back-120-0)[\[117\]](#refnote-120) On the night of 3 April, the then commander of the [Brazilian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Army "Brazilian Army") (EB), General [Eduardo Villas Bôas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduardo_Villas_B%C3%B4as?action=edit&redlink=1 "Eduardo Villas Bôas (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduardo%20Villas%20B%C3%B4as "pt:Eduardo Villas Bôas")\], posted two messages on his [Twitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter "Twitter") profile that were understood as a threat to the STF not to grant Lula's habeas corpus petition.[&#8202;](#ref-back-121-0)[\[118\]](#refnote-121)[&#8202;](#ref-back-122-0)[\[119\]](#refnote-122)[&#8202;](#ref-back-123-0)[\[120\]](#refnote-123) The following day, 4 April 2018, the Supreme Court, by a vote of 6 to 5, rejected Lula's habeas corpus petition, which would have allowed the former president to await his appeals in freedom after his conviction in the second instance.[&#8202;](#ref-back-124-0)[\[121\]](#refnote-124) Justice [Rosa Weber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosa_Weber "Rosa Weber")'s vote was decisive in the rejection.[&#8202;](#ref-back-125-0)[\[122\]](#refnote-125)[&#8202;](#ref-back-126-0)[\[123\]](#refnote-126)[&#8202;](#ref-back-127-0)[\[124\]](#refnote-127)

## Prison

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Prison")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Ato_pela_liberdade_de_Lula_em_Montes_Claros_-_MG.jpg/250px-Ato_pela_liberdade_de_Lula_em_Montes_Claros_-_MG.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ato_pela_liberdade_de_Lula_em_Montes_Claros_-_MG.jpg) Protestor holding a pro-Lula sign at a rally for the former president's freedom in [Montes Claros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montes_Claros "Montes Claros"), [Minas Gerais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minas_Gerais "Minas Gerais").

On 5 April 2018, after the Federal Supreme Court rejected the preventive habeas corpus, Federal Judge Sergio Moro ordered the imprisonment of Lula, who had been sentenced by the Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region (TRF-4) to 12 years and one month of imprisonment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:7-1)[\[13\]](#refnote-:7) The judge also defined special conditions for imprisonment due to the dignity of the office. According to the order, a reserved room was prepared at the [Federal Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Police_of_Brazil "Federal Police of Brazil") (PF) headquarters, where the former president would be separated from other prisoners, without any risk to his moral or physical integrity.[&#8202;](#ref-back-129-0)[\[126\]](#refnote-129) Moro also gave Lula a deadline to surrender to the Federal Police in Curitiba: by 5 p.m. on April 6. Experts said at the time that Moro's arrest warrant against Lula had atypical characteristics.[&#8202;](#ref-back-130-0)[\[127\]](#refnote-130) The former president's defense filed a new habeas corpus petition with the Superior Court of Justice to avoid his arrest, which was again denied.[&#8202;](#ref-back-131-0)[\[128\]](#refnote-131)

Lula decided not to report to the Federal Police headquarters in Curitiba,[&#8202;](#ref-back-132-0)[\[129\]](#refnote-132) and refused to leave the [ABC Metalworkers' Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_Metalworkers'_Union "ABC Metalworkers' Union") on April 6, 2018, when the arrest warrant issued by Moro for the former president expired.[&#8202;](#ref-back-133-0)[\[130\]](#refnote-133)[&#8202;](#ref-back-134-0)[\[131\]](#refnote-134) The Federal Police planned Lula's transportation in case he wanted to turn himself in at either the Federal Police headquarters in São Paulo or Curitiba.[&#8202;](#ref-back-135-0)[\[132\]](#refnote-135)

As Lula did not turn himself in by the deadline, rumors began to circulate that he could be placed in preventive detention.[&#8202;](#ref-back-136-0)[\[133\]](#refnote-136) After that, Lula decided to leave the union and turn himself in to the Federal Police the following day, 7 April, after a [Catholic mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_in_the_Catholic_Church "Mass in the Catholic Church") in honor of Marisa Letícia, who had died in February 2017.[&#8202;](#ref-back-137-0)[\[134\]](#refnote-137)[&#8202;](#ref-back-138-0)[\[135\]](#refnote-138)

On the morning of 7 April, Supreme Court Justice Edson Fachin denied yet another habeas corpus petition to prevent the former president's arrest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-139-0)[\[136\]](#refnote-139) Before leaving, Lula declared in a speech at the site that he intended to turn himself in to prove his innocence and avoid being considered a fugitive.[&#8202;](#ref-back-140-0)[\[137\]](#refnote-140) He also said that he was not afraid of Moro, that the real reason for his arrest was his [social policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_policy "Social policy"), and concluded by stating that his legacy would continue after his imprisonment, as his ideas were still alive: “The more they attack me, the more my relationship with the Brazilian people grows,” he added.[&#8202;](#ref-back-141-0)[\[138\]](#refnote-141)[&#8202;](#ref-back-142-0)[\[139\]](#refnote-142)

Finally, on 7 April 2018, Lula surrendered to the Federal Police, who were waiting for him outside the union headquarters, after attempting to leave and being prevented from doing so by activists on several occasions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-143-0)[\[140\]](#refnote-143)[&#8202;](#ref-back-144-0)[\[141\]](#refnote-144) From there, he was taken to Curitiba in a small aircraft.[&#8202;](#ref-back-145-0)[\[142\]](#refnote-145) Upon arriving in the capital of Paraná, he was taken by helicopter to his cell, a room at the Federal Police headquarters.[&#8202;](#ref-back-146-0)[\[143\]](#refnote-146) By order of federal judge Sergio Moro, responsible for the Car Wash Operation cases in the lower court, the former president was imprisoned in a special 15-square-meter room.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:8-0)[\[144\]](#refnote-:8) The space is located on the fourth floor of the Federal Police building and has a bed, desk, and private bathroom. A television was also authorized to be installed on site.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:8-1)[\[144\]](#refnote-:8) Lula's imprisonment gave rise to the [Free Lula movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Lula_movement "Free Lula movement").[&#8202;](#ref-back-148-0)[\[145\]](#refnote-148)[&#8202;](#ref-back-149-0)[\[146\]](#refnote-149)[&#8202;](#ref-back-150-0)[\[147\]](#refnote-150)

### Subsequent events

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Subsequent events")\]

On 11 April 2018, the Union of Federal Police Delegates of the State of Paraná (SinDPF/PR) requested, through an official letter sent to the Superintendence of the Federal Police, the transfer of former President Lula to a location that would offer better security conditions and that would not cause inconvenience or risks to the population and Federal Police employees.[&#8202;](#ref-back-151-0)[\[148\]](#refnote-151) According to the union, the location where the former president was being held was used to provide services to the general public, such as issuing passports and criminal records. “Thus, every day, hundreds of people who visit these facilities need, for various and relevant reasons, security and efficiency in service,” said the union.[&#8202;](#ref-back-152-0)[\[149\]](#refnote-152)[&#8202;](#ref-back-153-0)[\[150\]](#refnote-153)

On 10 May 2018, the second panel of the STF unanimously denied the former president's request for release in the virtual trial.[&#8202;](#ref-back-154-0)[\[151\]](#refnote-154) On May 15, STJ Minister [Felix Fischer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felix_Fischer?action=edit&redlink=1 "Felix Fischer (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felix%20Fischer "pt:Felix Fischer")\] rejected a new habeas corpus petition filed by Lula's defense team.[&#8202;](#ref-back-155-0)[\[152\]](#refnote-155)[&#8202;](#ref-back-156-0)[\[153\]](#refnote-156) On the same day, the triplex apartment in Guarujá attributed to Lula was sold at auction for 2.2 million reais.[&#8202;](#ref-back-157-0)[\[154\]](#refnote-157)

On 11 July 2018, the president of the STJ, Laurita Vaz, denied 143 requests for Lula's release.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:9-0)[\[155\]](#refnote-:9) These requests were not made by the former president's defense team, but by ordinary citizens.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:9-1)[\[155\]](#refnote-:9)

On 1 November 2018, shortly after the presidential elections, then-judge Sérgio Moro accepted an invitation from President-elect Jair Bolsonaro to serve as his Minister of Justice in the new administration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:10-0)[\[156\]](#refnote-:10) To take up the position of Minister of Justice, Moro stepped down as judge of Operation Car Wash.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:10-1)[\[156\]](#refnote-:10) Lula's defense filed a habeas corpus petition with the Supreme Court questioning the act.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:11-0)[\[157\]](#refnote-:11) In the petition, the defense argued that Moro's acceptance of the position of Minister of Justice under Bolsonaro demonstrated that the former judge had not acted impartially in Lula's case.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:11-1)[\[157\]](#refnote-:11)

On 19 December 2018, Supreme Court Justice Marco Aurélio Mello granted an [injunction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Injunction "Injunction") ordering that all prisoners convicted in the second instance and with appeals pending in court should be released.[&#8202;](#ref-back-161-0)[\[158\]](#refnote-161) The measure could have freed Lula from prison, and on the same day, the former president's defense team requested his release.[&#8202;](#ref-back-162-0)[\[159\]](#refnote-162) However, on the same day, the president of the STF, Dias Toffoli, suspended the injunction.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:12-0)[\[160\]](#refnote-:12) Toffoli argued that the issue of imprisonment after conviction in the second instance should be analyzed by the full STF in a session scheduled for 2019.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:12-1)[\[160\]](#refnote-:12)

On 29 January 2019, Lula's brother, Genival Inácio da Silva, known as Vavá, died of [cancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cancer "Cancer"), and the former president requested permission to attend the funeral and speak with family members.[&#8202;](#ref-back-164-0)[\[161\]](#refnote-164) Initially, federal judge [Carolina Lebbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolina_Lebbos?action=edit&redlink=1 "Carolina Lebbos (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolina%20Lebbos "pt:Carolina Lebbos")\] and TRF-4 judge Leandro Palsen denied permission to attend the funeral, but then STF President Dias Toffoli authorized Lula to leave prison to attend the ceremony and meet with his family.[&#8202;](#ref-back-165-0)[\[162\]](#refnote-165)[&#8202;](#ref-back-166-0)[\[163\]](#refnote-166) Despite the favorable decision, when Toffoli's authorization was issued, Vavá's body had already been buried. For this reason, Lula chose not to meet with his family and did not leave prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-167-0)[\[164\]](#refnote-167)[&#8202;](#ref-back-168-0)[\[165\]](#refnote-168)

On 21 February 2019, Supreme Court Justice Edson Fachin denied yet another request for Lula's release.[&#8202;](#ref-back-169-0)[\[166\]](#refnote-169)

On 1 March 2019, federal judge Carolina Lebbos authorized Lula's release from prison to travel to [São Bernardo do Campo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Bernardo_do_Campo "São Bernardo do Campo"), where he attended the funeral of his grandson, Arthur Araújo Lula da Silva, who died at the age of seven.[&#8202;](#ref-back-170-0)[\[167\]](#refnote-170)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:14-0)[\[168\]](#refnote-:14) Initially, it was reported that Arthur had died of [meningitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meningitis "Meningitis"), but medical tests confirmed that the cause of death was not meningitis, but rather a [generalized infection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sepsis "Sepsis").[&#8202;](#ref-back-172-0)[\[169\]](#refnote-172) After the ceremony, Lula returned to the Federal Police headquarters in Curitiba.[&#8202;](#ref-back-173-0)[\[170\]](#refnote-173)

On 10 May 2019, the former president's defense team requested that the STJ allow Lula to serve the remainder of his sentence under open conditions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-174-0)[\[171\]](#refnote-174) On June 4, 2019, the Federal Public Prosecutor's Office sent an opinion to the STJ, saying that Lula could serve the remainder of his sentence under a semi-open regime.[&#8202;](#ref-back-175-0)[\[172\]](#refnote-175) But on 23 September 2019, Lula declared, through a statement released by his lawyers, that he had decided not to request a change in his prison regime.[&#8202;](#ref-back-176-0)[\[173\]](#refnote-176)

On 25 June 2019, the second panel of the STF denied, by a vote of 4 to 1, another request for release made by Lula's defense team.[&#8202;](#ref-back-177-0)[\[174\]](#refnote-177)

On 7 August 2019, Carolina Lebbos authorized Lula's transfer to a prison in São Paulo.[&#8202;](#ref-back-178-0)[\[175\]](#refnote-178) On the same day, Judge Paulo Eduardo de Almeida Sorci, of the São Paulo state court, ruled that Lula should remain in the [Tremembé prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penitenci%C3%A1ria_II_de_Trememb%C3%A9?action=edit&redlink=1 "Penitenciária II de Tremembé (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penitenci%C3%A1ria%20II%20de%20Trememb%C3%A9 "pt:Penitenciária II de Tremembé")\], in the interior of São Paulo.[&#8202;](#ref-back-179-0)[\[176\]](#refnote-179) Hours later, the former president's defense team appealed to the STF against the transfer. The Supreme Court, by a vote of 10 to 1, suspended Lula's transfer to São Paulo until the Court could review a request for the former president's release.[&#8202;](#ref-back-180-0)[\[177\]](#refnote-180) In the request, the defense questioned the impartiality of Sergio Moro, who convicted Lula in the lower court.[&#8202;](#ref-back-181-0)[\[178\]](#refnote-181)

### Subsequent convictions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Subsequent convictions")\]

#### Third instance

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Third instance")\]

In November 2018, when Lula was already in prison, the appeal filed by his lawyers against his conviction in the Guarujá triplex case reached the Superior Court of Justice (STJ).[&#8202;](#ref-back-:13-0)[\[179\]](#refnote-:13) The defense alleged that there had been several violations of rights during the proceedings and requested Lula's acquittal or the annulment of his conviction. Justice Felix Fischer initially denied this request.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:13-1)[\[179\]](#refnote-:13) The defense then appealed to the Fifth Panel of the STJ.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:13-2)[\[179\]](#refnote-:13)

On 23 April 2019, the Fifth Panel reviewed the appeal and, by unanimous decision, upheld Lula's conviction in the triplex case. However, the justices reduced the sentence to 8 years, 10 months, and 20 days in prison, in addition to reducing the amount of the fine that the former president would have to pay as compensation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-183-0)[\[180\]](#refnote-183) Justices Felix Fischer, [Jorge Mussi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Mussi?action=edit&redlink=1 "Jorge Mussi (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge%20Mussi "pt:Jorge Mussi")\], [Reynaldo Soares da Fonseca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reynaldo_Soares_da_Fonseca?action=edit&redlink=1 "Reynaldo Soares da Fonseca (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reynaldo%20Soares%20da%20Fonseca "pt:Reynaldo Soares da Fonseca")\], and [Ribeiro Dantas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ribeiro_Dantas?action=edit&redlink=1 "Ribeiro Dantas (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ribeiro%20Dantas%20(magistrado) "pt:Ribeiro Dantas (magistrado)")\] participated in the trial.[&#8202;](#ref-back-184-0)[\[181\]](#refnote-184) Justice [Joel Ilan Paciornik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joel_Ilan_Paciornik?action=edit&redlink=1 "Joel Ilan Paciornik (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joel%20Ilan%20Paciornik "pt:Joel Ilan Paciornik")\] did not participate in the decision because he recused himself.[&#8202;](#ref-back-185-0)[\[182\]](#refnote-185)

#### Farm in Atibaia

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Farm in Atibaia")\]

On 6 February 2019, Lula was sentenced by Judge Gabriela Hardt, Moro's replacement, to 12 years and 11 months in prison in another case involving a ranch in the city of Atibaia, in the interior of São Paulo.[&#8202;](#ref-back-186-0)[\[183\]](#refnote-186) In that case, Lula was accused of receiving kickbacks from OAS and Odebrecht through renovations to the ranch.[&#8202;](#ref-back-187-0)[\[184\]](#refnote-187) On 27 November 2019, the Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region (TRF-4) in Porto Alegre ruled on Lula's appeal in the second instance and unanimously upheld the conviction in the ranch case and increased the sentence to 17 years, one month, and 10 days in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-188-0)[\[185\]](#refnote-188) The former president's defense considered the conviction unfair and arbitrary. In an appeal, it said that the proceedings and the merits of the case had not been resolved, as the conviction stated that Lula had been convicted on the basis of the assertion that he "was the main orchestrator...of a corruption scheme... at Petrobras," contradicting the final ruling handed down by the 12th Federal Court of Brasília, which acquitted the former president of this conviction.[&#8202;](#ref-back-189-0)[\[186\]](#refnote-189) The defense also complained that Gabriela Hardt copied parts of Sergio Moro's ruling in the Guarujá triplex case to convict Lula in the Atibaia ranch case.[&#8202;](#ref-back-190-0)[\[187\]](#refnote-190)[&#8202;](#ref-back-191-0)[\[188\]](#refnote-191) Hardt admitted that he used the triplex sentence as a model, but denied any injustice, claiming that it is common for judges to use other sentences as models and that, despite having used the triplex sentence as a model, the reasoning is different.[&#8202;](#ref-back-192-0)[\[189\]](#refnote-192) On 13 November 2019, the TRF-4 overturned another ruling by Gabriela Hardt (unrelated to Lula) on the grounds that the judge had copied the ruling.[&#8202;](#ref-back-193-0)[\[190\]](#refnote-193)

### Appearances after arrest

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: Appearances after arrest")\]

While in prison, Lula was seen in public on few occasions. His first public appearance after his arrest was on June 5, 2018, when the former president testified before Judge [Marcelo Bretas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_Bretas?action=edit&redlink=1 "Marcelo Bretas (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo%20Bretas "pt:Marcelo Bretas")\] in a criminal case investigating alleged vote buying for the selection of Rio de Janeiro as the host city for the [2016 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Summer_Olympics "2016 Summer Olympics").[&#8202;](#ref-back-194-0)[\[191\]](#refnote-194) Lula testified via videoconference.[&#8202;](#ref-back-195-0)[\[192\]](#refnote-195) The second appearance was on 14 November 2018, when the former president testified before Judge Gabriela Hardt in the case involving the ranch in Atibaia. Lula was temporarily released from prison in Curitiba to testify.[&#8202;](#ref-back-196-0)[\[193\]](#refnote-196) The third appearance was on 1 March 2019, when Lula was allowed to attend his grandson's funeral in São Bernardo do Campo.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:14-1)[\[168\]](#refnote-:14) During the ceremony, Lula only spoke in an area reserved for family and friends.[&#8202;](#ref-back-197-0)[\[194\]](#refnote-197) According to people present at the scene, Lula promised his grandson that he would prove his innocence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-198-0)[\[195\]](#refnote-198) The fourth appearance was on 26 April 2019, when the former president gave his first interview in prison to the newspapers _Folha de S.Paulo_ and _[El País](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Pa%C3%ADs "El País")_. [&#8202;](#ref-back-:35-0)[\[196\]](#refnote-:35)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:36-0)[\[197\]](#refnote-:36)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:15-0)[\[198\]](#refnote-:15)[&#8202;](#ref-back-202-0)[\[199\]](#refnote-202)[&#8202;](#ref-back-203-0)[\[200\]](#refnote-203)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:37-0)[\[201\]](#refnote-:37) The fifth appearance was on 10 May 2019, when Lula's second interview in prison, conducted for the [BBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC "BBC"), was aired.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:16-0)[\[202\]](#refnote-:16) On 15 May 2019, Lula gave his third interview in prison, this time to journalist [Glenn Greenwald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Greenwald "Glenn Greenwald") of _[The Intercept Brasil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Intercept_Brasil "The Intercept Brasil")_. The interview was made available in full on the internet on 21 May 2019.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:17-0)[\[203\]](#refnote-:17) After that, for the rest of his time in prison, Lula appeared only in interviews he gave in prison.

### Interview requests

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: Interview requests")\]

On 11 July 2018, Judge Carolina Moura Lebbos, responsible for enforcing Lula's sentence, denied a request by media outlets to interview him in prison. Among the outlets that made the request were [_UOL_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universo_Online "Universo Online"), _Folha de S.Paulo_, [SBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistema_Brasileiro_de_Televis%C3%A3o "Sistema Brasileiro de Televisão"), [Rede TV!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RedeTV! "RedeTV!"), and _[Diário do Centro do Mundo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Di%C3%A1rio_do_Centro_do_Mundo?action=edit&redlink=1 "Diário do Centro do Mundo (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Di%C3%A1rio%20do%20Centro%20do%20Mundo "pt:Diário do Centro do Mundo")\]_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:35-1)[\[196\]](#refnote-:35) Journalists [Mônica Bergamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%B4nica_Bergamo?action=edit&redlink=1 "Mônica Bergamo (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%B4nica%20Bergamo "pt:Mônica Bergamo")\], from _Folha de S.Paulo_, and [Florestan Fernandes Júnior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florestan_Fernandes_J%C3%BAnior?action=edit&redlink=1 "Florestan Fernandes Júnior (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florestan%20Fernandes%20J%C3%BAnior "pt:Florestan Fernandes Júnior")\], from _El País_, filed a constitutional complaint with the Federal Supreme Court against Judge Lebbos' decision.[&#8202;](#ref-back-207-0)[\[204\]](#refnote-207) On the morning of 28 September 2018, Supreme Court Justice Ricardo Lewandowski authorized Lula to give interviews in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:35-2)[\[196\]](#refnote-:35) Hours later, however, the [New Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Party_(Brazil) "New Party (Brazil)") requested the suspension of the injunction, and Supreme Court Justice Luiz Fux suspended Lewandowski's decision and prohibited Lula from giving an interview to _Folha de S.Paulo_. If the interview had already been conducted, it could not be published.[&#8202;](#ref-back-208-0)[\[205\]](#refnote-208) The New Party also filed a lawsuit to prevent _El País_ from interviewing Lula.[&#8202;](#ref-back-209-0)[\[206\]](#refnote-209) On 1 October 2018, Lewandowski reaffirmed his decision, but the president of the STF, Dias Toffoli, decided to uphold Fux's decision.[&#8202;](#ref-back-210-0)[\[207\]](#refnote-210) Fux's decision was criticized by lawyers.[&#8202;](#ref-back-211-0)[\[208\]](#refnote-211)

Two days later, Ricardo Lewandowski once again authorized Lula to give interviews in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-212-0)[\[209\]](#refnote-212) On the same day, Dias Toffoli overturned the decision again and kept Lula banned from giving interviews from prison, but warned that the matter could be brought before the full Supreme Court at a later date.[&#8202;](#ref-back-213-0)[\[210\]](#refnote-213) On 4 December 2018, Lewandowski asked Toffoli to authorize Lula to give interviews, since the argument for the ban was that the former president's interviews could influence the 2018 electoral process, but that risk no longer existed because the elections had already been held.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:36-1)[\[197\]](#refnote-:36) On 18 April 2019, Dias Toffoli revoked Fux's decision and authorized Lula to give interviews in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:37-1)[\[201\]](#refnote-:37) On 26 April 2019, Lula gave his first interview in prison to the newspapers _Folha de S.Paulo_ and _El País_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-214-0)[\[211\]](#refnote-214)

After Folha and El País won their court case, Lula gave several other interviews during his period of restricted freedom.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:37-2)[\[201\]](#refnote-:37) Below are all the interviews given by the former president during this period:
