| 19 | 09/10/19 | Paulo Dentinho | _[RTP1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTP1 "RTP1")_ | [&#8202;](#ref-back-230-0)[\[227\]](#refnote-230) |
| 20 | 16/10/19 | Flávio Costa and [Leonardo Sakamoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_Sakamoto?action=edit&redlink=1 "Leonardo Sakamoto (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo%20Sakamoto "pt:Leonardo Sakamoto")\] | _[Universo Online](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universo_Online "Universo Online")_ _(two parts published)_ | [&#8202;](#ref-back-231-0)[\[228\]](#refnote-231) |
| 21 | 23/10/19 | Nina Fideles and Beatriz Pasqualino | _[Brasil de Fato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brasil_de_Fato "Brasil de Fato")_ | [&#8202;](#ref-back-232-0)[\[229\]](#refnote-232) |
| 22 | 30/10/19 | Marina Amaral and Thiago Domenici | _[Agência Pública](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ag%C3%AAncia_P%C3%BAblica "Agência Pública")_ | [&#8202;](#ref-back-233-0)[\[230\]](#refnote-233) |

### Loss of benefits

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=23 "Edit section: Loss of benefits")\]

On 17 May 2018, Federal Judge Haroldo Nader, of the 6th District Court of [Campinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campinas "Campinas"), granted an emergency injunction in a _[actio popularis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Actio_popularis "Actio popularis")_ lawsuit and ordered Lula's benefits as former president to be cut off.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:18-0)[\[231\]](#refnote-:18) The magistrate's central argument is that Lula would be safer in the Lava Jato prison in Curitiba than he would be if he were free.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:18-1)[\[231\]](#refnote-:18) The magistrate highlighted the unnecessary mobilization of agents to accompany Lula wherever he went. “It is also absolutely unnecessary to have two vehicles with drivers available to someone whose right to movement is restricted to the Federal Police building in Curitiba and controlled by prison guards,” said the judge.[&#8202;](#ref-back-235-0)[\[232\]](#refnote-235)[&#8202;](#ref-back-236-0)[\[233\]](#refnote-236)[&#8202;](#ref-back-237-0)[\[234\]](#refnote-237) The lawsuit was filed by Rubens Nunes, coordinator of the [Free Brazil Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Brazil_Movement "Free Brazil Movement") (MBL).[&#8202;](#ref-back-238-0)[\[235\]](#refnote-238)[&#8202;](#ref-back-239-0)[\[236\]](#refnote-239) On 29 May 2018, Judge Marcelo Saraiva, of the Federal Regional Court of the 3rd Region, upheld an appeal and restored Lula's benefits as former president of the Republic, such as advisors, drivers, security guards, and an official car. The magistrate emphasized that these benefits constitute rights and privileges granted by law to former presidents and are not perks.[&#8202;](#ref-back-240-0)[\[237\]](#refnote-240)

### Prison visits

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=24 "Edit section: Prison visits")\]

When Lula was arrested, only his family and lawyers were allowed to visit him in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-241-0)[\[238\]](#refnote-241) Judge Sergio Moro ruled that lawyers could visit him every day and his family only on Wednesdays. However, a few days after his arrest, it was decided that Lula would receive family visits on a day other than Wednesday. The intention was to prevent his family from meeting with the families of his accusers, since Wednesday is the usual visiting day.[&#8202;](#ref-back-242-0)[\[239\]](#refnote-242) The former president requested that his family visit him on Thursdays, and the Federal Police agreed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:19-0)[\[240\]](#refnote-:19) Thus, Lula began to receive visits from his family on Thursdays, while his lawyers could visit him every day.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:19-1)[\[240\]](#refnote-:19) On 10 April 2018, a delegation of nine governors and three senators attempted to visit Lula in prison, but the visit was not authorized.[&#8202;](#ref-back-244-0)[\[241\]](#refnote-244) On 3 May 2018, Lula received a visit from Senator [Gleisi Hoffmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gleisi_Hoffmann "Gleisi Hoffmann") and former minister and former governor of [Bahia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahia "Bahia"), [Jaques Wagner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaques_Wagner "Jaques Wagner").[&#8202;](#ref-back-245-0)[\[242\]](#refnote-245)

In April 2018, he was visited by Argentine [Nobel Peace Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Peace_Prize "Nobel Peace Prize") winner [Adolfo Pérez Esquivel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolfo_P%C3%A9rez_Esquivel "Adolfo Pérez Esquivel").[&#8202;](#ref-back-246-0)[\[243\]](#refnote-246) In June 2018, Argentine lawyer [Juan Grabois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Grabois "Juan Grabois") attempted to visit Lula in prison, but his visit was not authorized.[&#8202;](#ref-back-247-0)[\[244\]](#refnote-247) At the time, Grabois stated that he intended to give Lula a [rosary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosary "Rosary") blessed by [Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis "Pope Francis").[&#8202;](#ref-back-248-0)[\[245\]](#refnote-248) After that, information was posted on the internet that the Pope had sent a rosary as a gift to Lula, but the [Vatican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vatican_City "Vatican City") denied the information.[&#8202;](#ref-back-249-0)[\[246\]](#refnote-249) The Vatican said that the rosary in question had only been blessed by the Pope, it was not a gift from him to Lula, and that Grabois' visit was personal, not on behalf of the Pope.[&#8202;](#ref-back-250-0)[\[247\]](#refnote-250) On 2 August 2018, former minister [Celso Amorim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celso_Amorim "Celso Amorim") delivered a message from Pope Francis to Lula.[&#8202;](#ref-back-251-0)[\[248\]](#refnote-251)

On 25 January 2019, Judge Carolina Lebbos ruled that former mayor [Fernando Haddad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Haddad "Fernando Haddad") could only visit Lula on Thursdays. Haddad had joined Lula's defense team and, until then, had been able to visit the former president any day of the week.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:20-0)[\[249\]](#refnote-:20) The judge argued that Haddad's power of attorney to represent the former president had lost its meaning, since Haddad was defending Lula's rights as a pre-candidate for the presidency in 2018, but the elections had already passed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:20-1)[\[249\]](#refnote-:20) The judge also ruled that the former president could no longer receive visits from religious leaders every Monday, as had been authorized in 2018.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:21-0)[\[250\]](#refnote-:21) Thus, Lula could only receive visits from religious leaders once a month.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:21-1)[\[250\]](#refnote-:21) On 1 March 2019, Fernando Haddad obtained a new power of attorney to represent Lula in his defense and, as a result, was once again able to visit the former president regularly in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-254-0)[\[251\]](#refnote-254)

On 17 September 2019, Lula received a visit in prison from a commission from the National Human Rights Council, which wanted to hear him about possible violations in Operation Car Wash proceedings, including the very case that convicted him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-255-0)[\[252\]](#refnote-255) In October 2019, Lula received visits from [Richard Trumka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Trumka "Richard Trumka") and Pepe Alvarez, respectively the president of the [AFL-CIO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL-CIO "AFL-CIO"), the largest trade union federation in the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States"), and the secretary-general of Spain's largest trade union federation, the [Unión General de Trabajadores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uni%C3%B3n_General_de_Trabajadores "Unión General de Trabajadores") (UGT).[&#8202;](#ref-back-256-0)[\[253\]](#refnote-256)[&#8202;](#ref-back-257-0)[\[254\]](#refnote-257) During this visit by union leaders, Lula received the [George Meany-Lane Kirkland Human Rights Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Meany-Lane_Kirkland_Human_Rights_Award "George Meany-Lane Kirkland Human Rights Award") from the AFL-CIO.[&#8202;](#ref-back-258-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-258)

During his 500 days in prison, Lula received more visitors, such as American actor [Danny Glover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Glover "Danny Glover"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-:23-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-:23) singers [Chico Buarque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chico_Buarque "Chico Buarque") and [Martinho da Vila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martinho_da_Vila "Martinho da Vila"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-260-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-260) and former Uruguayan president [José Mujica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mujica "José Mujica").[&#8202;](#ref-back-261-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-261) Among others are Portuguese sociologist [Boaventura de Sousa Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boaventura_de_Sousa_Santos "Boaventura de Sousa Santos"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-262-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-262) German [Martin Schulz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Schulz "Martin Schulz") of the [Social Democratic Party of Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Democratic_Party_of_Germany "Social Democratic Party of Germany") (SPD) and former president of the [European Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Parliament "European Parliament"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-263-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-263) Brazilian theologian [Leonardo Boff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_Boff "Leonardo Boff"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-264-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-264) American intellectual [Noam Chomsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noam_Chomsky "Noam Chomsky"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-265-0)[\[262\]](#refnote-265) Brazilian Buddhist leader [Coen Rōshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coen_R%C5%8Dshi?action=edit&redlink=1 "Coen Rōshi (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coen%20R%C5%8Dshi "pt:Coen Rōshi")\],[&#8202;](#ref-back-266-0)[\[263\]](#refnote-266) former Italian Prime Minister [Massimo D'Alema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massimo_D'Alema "Massimo D'Alema"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-:24-0)[\[264\]](#refnote-:24) former governor of the Federal District of Mexico [Cuauhtémoc Cárdenas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuauht%C3%A9moc_C%C3%A1rdenas "Cuauhtémoc Cárdenas"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-:24-1)[\[264\]](#refnote-:24) and former Brazilian President [Dilma Rousseff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilma_Rousseff "Dilma Rousseff").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:23-1)[\[256\]](#refnote-:23)

### United Nations Human Rights Committee

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=25 "Edit section: United Nations Human Rights Committee")\]

On 17 August, the United Nations Human Rights Committee requested that Brazil guarantee Lula the full exercise of his political rights, including access to the media and members of his party. The Committee also requested that he not be barred from participating in the 2018 presidential election in Brazil until appeals to higher courts had been judged impartially.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:22-1)[\[25\]](#refnote-:22)

The Brazilian government, through the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, responded on the same day that the Committee would be composed of individuals who do not represent countries and that the request would not have binding effect, being only a recommendation, and informing that it had forwarded the Committee's decision to the Judiciary.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:26-1)[\[19\]](#refnote-:26)

On 23 August, Sarah Cleveland, the Committee's vice-chair, emphasized that the measures are not recommendations and that Brazil must comply with the Committee's decisions, whose actions are based on commitments made by the country to become a member of the community of states that respect human rights. She explained that:[&#8202;](#ref-back-:25-1)[\[18\]](#refnote-:25)

> The precautionary measures issued by the UN Human Rights Committee are not recommendations. They are legally binding and impose an international legal obligation on Brazil to comply with them.

The Workers' Party even registered Lula's candidacy for the Brazilian presidency in the 2018 elections.[&#8202;](#ref-back-268-0)[\[265\]](#refnote-268) However, on 31 August 2018, the Superior Electoral Court, by a vote of 6 to 1, barred Lula's candidacy based on the [Clean Record Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_Record_Act "Clean Record Act"), which determines that those convicted by a collegiate body are ineligible for eight years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:27-0)[\[266\]](#refnote-:27) This is the case with Lula, since the TRF-4, which convicted him in the second instance, is a collegiate body.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:27-1)[\[266\]](#refnote-:27) The majority of the TSE collegiate body understood that the decision of the UN Human Rights Committee was not binding and did not have the normative power to annul the ineligibility linked to the Clean Record Act, and that the precautionary measure of the UN Committee based on the [International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Covenant_on_Civil_and_Political_Rights "International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights") (ICCPR) could not be accepted because, in addition to having been issued by only two of the eighteen members of the Committee, there was “no justification regarding the imminent risk of irreparable damage to the right to run for election, provided for in Article 25 of the ICCPR.” [&#8202;](#ref-back-270-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-270) Furthermore, the Covenant “is an intermediate norm and cannot contradict the original text of the [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Brazil "Constitution of Brazil").” The only vote in favor of the candidacy, cast by Minister Edson Fachin, considered that, although the ICCPR is not effective in Brazil due to the lack of an executive decree, this could not prevent its validity in the national territory because “its provisions have supra-legal effect, being hierarchically below the Constitution, but above ordinary legislation.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-:27-2)[\[266\]](#refnote-:27)

### Preliminary injunction to release Lula denied

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=26 "Edit section: Preliminary injunction to release Lula denied")\]

Controversy arose among TRF-4 judges on Sunday, 8 July 2018, over whether to release Lula or keep him in prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-271-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-271) The following is a chronology of events, all of which occurred on the same day:[&#8202;](#ref-back-:29-0)[\[269\]](#refnote-:29)

- At around 9 a.m., Federal Judge [Rogério Favreto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rog%C3%A9rio_Favreto?action=edit&redlink=1 "Rogério Favreto (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rog%C3%A9rio%20Favreto "pt:Rogério Favreto")\], acting as duty judge for the Federal Regional Court of the 4th Region (TRF-4), ruled on a request for a preliminary injunction in a _habeas corpus_ case, granting Lula's release;[&#8202;](#ref-back-273-0)[\[270\]](#refnote-273)
- Later, around noon, Judge Sergio Moro, summoned as the coercive authority, denied coercion and replied that he had only complied with a previous ruling by the 8th panel of the TRF-4, stating that he did not have the power to determine Lula's imprisonment or release. Understanding that the judge on duty would not have the authority to grant freedom to a convicted person tried by a collegiate body, Moro requested guidance from Judge [João Pedro Gebran Neto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Pedro_Gebran_Neto?action=edit&redlink=1 "João Pedro Gebran Neto (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o%20Pedro%20Gebran%20Neto "pt:João Pedro Gebran Neto")\];[&#8202;](#ref-back-274-0)[\[271\]](#refnote-274)
- Forty minutes later, Favreto issued a new order, reiterating his decision to release him;[&#8202;](#ref-back-275-0)[\[272\]](#refnote-275)
- At around 1 p.m., the Federal Public Prosecutor's Office requested reconsideration of the preliminary injunction granted at 9 a.m. by Favreto;[&#8202;](#ref-back-276-0)[\[273\]](#refnote-276)
- At around 2:20 p.m., Federal Judge João Pedro Gebran Neto, who was the presiding judge in the Car Wash cases in the second instance, assumed jurisdiction over the _habeas corpus_ petition and ruled that Favreto's decision should not be enforced;[&#8202;](#ref-back-:29-1)[\[269\]](#refnote-:29)
- At around 4 p.m., despite the intervention, Judge Favreto again ordered the release of former President Lula, arguing that he had deliberated on new facts, granting a period of one hour for compliance;[&#8202;](#ref-back-277-0)[\[274\]](#refnote-277)
- At around 6 p.m., the Federal Public Prosecutor's Office spoke up again, requesting that the president of the Court resolve the disagreement;[&#8202;](#ref-back-:29-2)[\[269\]](#refnote-:29)
- A little over thirty minutes after 7 p.m., the president of the TRF-4, Federal Judge [Carlos Eduardo Thompson Flores Lenz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Eduardo_Thompson_Flores_Lenz?action=edit&redlink=1 "Carlos Eduardo Thompson Flores Lenz (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos%20Eduardo%20Thompson%20Flores%20Lenz "pt:Carlos Eduardo Thompson Flores Lenz")\], ruled that Lula should remain in prison and that the case should be returned to the judge responsible for the Lava Jato cases in the Court, Federal Judge Gebran Neto.[&#8202;](#ref-back-278-0)[\[275\]](#refnote-278)

Excerpt from Thompson Flores' ruling that resolved the issue in the TRF-4:[&#8202;](#ref-back-279-0)[\[276\]](#refnote-279)

> In this equation, considering that the matter discussed in the habeas corpus does not challenge analysis under the judicial duty system and given the right of the Federal Reporting Judge to use the institution of assumption of jurisdiction to preserve his own jurisdiction (Internal Regulations/TRF-4R, art. 202), I order the return of the case files to the Office of Federal Judge João Pedro Gebran Neto, as well as the maintenance of the decision he rendered in event 17.

Subsequently, _Veja_ magazine published on 11 August 2018, that “Gebran Neto admitted to friends that he ignored the letter of the law when he ruled against Lula's release” at the time, “disregarding the jurisdiction of the judge on duty.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-280-0)[\[277\]](#refnote-280) The publication of the news caused the [hashtag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashtag "Hashtag") “#GebranForaDaLei” (#GebranOutlaw) to remain among the [Trending topics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trending_topics "Trending topics") in Brazil on Twitter for most of the day, generating accusations of bias and disrespect for institutions on the part of the judge.[&#8202;](#ref-back-281-0)[\[278\]](#refnote-281)

## Release from custody

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=27 "Edit section: Release from custody")\]

After 580 days imprisoned in the Federal Police jail in Curitiba, Lula was released on 8 November 2019, one day after the Federal Supreme Court ruled that imprisonment after conviction in the second instance was unconstitutional.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:28-1)[\[6\]](#refnote-:28)[&#8202;](#ref-back-282-0)[\[279\]](#refnote-282) The decision for immediate release was made by Judge Danilo Pereira Júnior, of the 12th Federal Court of Curitiba, after a request by lawyer [Cristiano Zanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristiano_Zanin "Cristiano Zanin").[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[280\]](#refnote-283) Accompanying Lula's release from prison were, in addition to his lawyer Zanin, members of the Workers' Party, including Fernando Haddad, Gliesi Hoffman, [Wadih Damous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadih_Damous?action=edit&redlink=1 "Wadih Damous (page does not exist)")\[ [pt](https://pt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadih%20Damous "pt:Wadih Damous")\], and [Lindbergh Farias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lindbergh_Farias "Lindbergh Farias").[&#8202;](#ref-back-284-0)[\[281\]](#refnote-284)[&#8202;](#ref-back-285-0)[\[282\]](#refnote-285)[&#8202;](#ref-back-286-0)[\[283\]](#refnote-286) It was also Lula's first public appearance with [Rosângela Lula da Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ros%C3%A2ngela_Lula_da_Silva "Rosângela Lula da Silva"), his new girlfriend at the time and future [first lady of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Lady_of_Brazil "First Lady of Brazil").[&#8202;](#ref-back-287-0)[\[284\]](#refnote-287)[&#8202;](#ref-back-288-0)[\[285\]](#refnote-288)

Lula said that during the 580 days he spent in prison, he read more than forty books.[&#8202;](#ref-back-289-0)[\[286\]](#refnote-289)[&#8202;](#ref-back-290-0)[\[287\]](#refnote-290)

## Repercussions of the arrest

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=28 "Edit section: Repercussions of the arrest")\]

Main article: [Free Lula movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Lula_movement "Free Lula movement")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Pixuleco3.jpg/250px-Pixuleco3.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Pixuleco3.jpg) A doll used in demonstrations depicts Lula as a prisoner. The inflatable doll was called 'Pixuleco', in reference to the Federal Police's Operation Pixuleco. Lula's imprisonment was one of the demands made in the protests against [Dilma Rousseff's government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilma_Rousseff's_government "Dilma Rousseff's government").

### Press

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=29 "Edit section: Press")\]

- _[The New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times "The New York Times")_: highlighted the turnaround that Lula's imprisonment represented for the Brazilian presidential elections. The American newspaper quoted Lula's speech, in which he promised to regain control of Brazil's destiny and prioritize policies to reduce inequality in the country. The article also highlighted the political division in the country, “with some Brazilians setting off fireworks and honking their horns while others lamented the arrest.” In an editorial, the newspaper concluded that “Despite the success of Operation Car Wash, nothing has been done to fix the judicial system. The danger of a shift toward populism and political radicalization is obvious.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-291-0)[\[288\]](#refnote-291)
- [_Al Jazeera_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_Media_Network "Al Jazeera Media Network"): published an extensive article, emphasizing that the former Brazilian president continues to assert his innocence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-292-0)[\[289\]](#refnote-292)
- _[Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde "Le Monde")_: published “the fall of the icon of the Brazilian left” and quoted the former president's speech to his supporters.[&#8202;](#ref-back-293-0)[\[290\]](#refnote-293)
- _[O Globo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Globo "O Globo")_: in an editorial, the newspaper stated that “Lula's imprisonment reinforces the rule of law” and affirmed that “more than ever, the republican maxim that justice is for all applies.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-294-0)[\[291\]](#refnote-294)
- _[Folha de S.Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folha_de_S.Paulo "Folha de S.Paulo")_: In an editorial entitled “Obey the law,” the newspaper praised the decision and added, “apart from a small group of militants, driven by nonconformity or hatred, society is mature and calm enough to resolve its conflicts and problems. Corruption is one of them, and Brazilian progress, in this regard, is confirmed once again.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-295-0)[\[292\]](#refnote-295)

### Presidential candidates

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=30 "Edit section: Presidential candidates")\]

Repercussions among the candidates for the presidency of the republic in the 2018 presidential election in Brazil, which took place not long after Lula's imprisonment:

#### Favorable

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=31 "Edit section: Favorable")\]

- [Marina Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marina_Silva "Marina Silva"), presidential candidate for [Sustainability Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainability_Network "Sustainability Network") (REDE), said that the former president's conviction shows that “no one is above the law.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-296-0)[\[293\]](#refnote-296) Still on the subject of imprisonment, Marina stated that "Today is a difficult day, because we have a situation where a former president of the Republic is about to serve his sentence for the crimes he committed. But this indicates that we are beginning a process of changing the law that should apply to everyone."[&#8202;](#ref-back-297-0)[\[294\]](#refnote-297)
- [Geraldo Alckmin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geraldo_Alckmin "Geraldo Alckmin"), presidential candidate for the [Brazilian Social Democracy Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Social_Democracy_Party "Brazilian Social Democracy Party") (PSDB) defended the arrest of the former president and said that “no one is above the law.” “In public life, everyone has a duty to be accountable,” Alckimin said.[&#8202;](#ref-back-298-0)[\[295\]](#refnote-298)
- [Alvaro Dias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alvaro_Dias "Alvaro Dias"), presidential candidate for [Podemos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podemos_(Brazil) "Podemos (Brazil)") (PODE), stated that the Clean Record Act benefits from Lula's imprisonment, and praised the arrest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-299-0)[\[296\]](#refnote-299) He also criticized “those who wave their red flags in the streets of the country calling for freedom for the leader of the criminal organization.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-300-0)[\[297\]](#refnote-300)
- [Jair Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro "Jair Bolsonaro"), presidential candidate for the [Social Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Liberal_Party_(Brazil) "Social Liberal Party (Brazil)") (PSL), defended the imprisonment of the former president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-301-0)[\[298\]](#refnote-301)
- [Vera Lúcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vera_L%C3%BAcia_Salgado "Vera Lúcia Salgado"), presidential candidate for the [Unified Socialist Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Socialist_Workers'_Party "United Socialist Workers' Party") (PSTU), expressed her support and stated that “Lula was reaping what he had sown.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-302-0)[\[299\]](#refnote-302)

#### Opponents

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=32 "Edit section: Opponents")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Manuela%2C_Lula_e_Boulos_em_Curitiba.jpg/250px-Manuela%2C_Lula_e_Boulos_em_Curitiba.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Manuela,_Lula_e_Boulos_em_Curitiba.jpg) Manuela d'Ávila, Lula, and Guilherme Boulos at a protest against the former president's imprisonment in Curitiba.

- [Manuela d'Ávila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuela_d'%C3%81vila "Manuela d'Ávila"), presidential candidate for the [Communist Party of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Brazil "Communist Party of Brazil") (PCdoB), has spoken out against Lula's imprisonment and actively participates in “Pro-Lula” demonstrations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-303-0)[\[300\]](#refnote-303)
- [Guilherme Boulos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guilherme_Boulos "Guilherme Boulos"), presidential candidate for the [Socialism and Liberty Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_and_Liberty_Party "Socialism and Liberty Party") (PSOL), took a stand against Lula's imprisonment and criticized Ciro Gomes for his absence from the demonstration before the former president's arrest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-304-0)[\[301\]](#refnote-304)
- [Ciro Gomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciro_Gomes "Ciro Gomes"), presidential candidate for the [Democratic Labour Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Labour_Party_(Brazil) "Democratic Labour Party (Brazil)") (PDT), first said that society had become “suspicious” that the law was only harsh on the PT and Lula. “It seems that harsh laws only apply to one side in Brazil. Harsh laws are very good if they apply to everyone,” said Ciro.[&#8202;](#ref-back-305-0)[\[302\]](#refnote-305) Ciro then said that the sentence that convicted Lula was weak and unfair.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:30-1)[\[106\]](#refnote-:30)
- [Fernando Haddad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Haddad "Fernando Haddad"), the PT's presidential candidate who replaced Lula as the party's candidate, said he would seek all legal means to free the former president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-306-0)[\[303\]](#refnote-306)[&#8202;](#ref-back-307-0)[\[304\]](#refnote-307)[&#8202;](#ref-back-308-0)[\[305\]](#refnote-308) But he denied that, if elected, he would [presidential pardon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pardon "Pardon") to Lula, stated in an interview with [Central Brasileira de Notícias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Brasileira_de_Not%C3%ADcias "Central Brasileira de Notícias").[&#8202;](#ref-back-309-0)[\[306\]](#refnote-309)
- [José Maria Eymael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Maria_Eymael "José Maria Eymael"), presidential candidate for the [Christian Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Democracy_(Brazil) "Christian Democracy (Brazil)") (DC), said that Lula's imprisonment is “unconstitutional.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-310-0)[\[307\]](#refnote-310)

### Foreign parties

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=33 "Edit section: Foreign parties")\]

The Spanish party [Podemos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podemos_(Spanish_political_party) "Podemos (Spanish political party)") criticized the arrest of the former president. One of the party's representatives in the European Parliament stated that “Brazil does not meet the minimum democratic conditions.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-:31-0)[\[308\]](#refnote-:31) The party stated that the process at all stages “has been based on irregularities.” It also stated that the process is linked to the interests of the [elite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elite "Elite") who are trying to prevent the PT from returning to the presidency of the country. The party asked the European Parliament to suspend negotiations with [Mercosur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercosur "Mercosur").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:31-1)[\[308\]](#refnote-:31)[&#8202;](#ref-back-312-0)[\[309\]](#refnote-312)

The Colombian party [Common Alternative Revolutionary Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commons_(Colombian_political_party) "Commons (Colombian political party)") (FARC) declared its solidarity “with comrade and former Brazilian president Lula, rejecting the decision taken against him by the Brazilian Supreme Court and demanding all political and judicial guarantees under the Brazilian constitution, its internal order, and international law.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-313-0)[\[310\]](#refnote-313) In a statement, [Raúl Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra%C3%BAl_Castro "Raúl Castro"), first secretary of the [Communist Party of Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Cuba "Communist Party of Cuba") (PCC) and former Cuban president, reiterated that Cuba will always stand by the Brazilian people. Read the full message: “To our comrade Lula, victim of political persecution and coup maneuvers, we express our solidarity in the face of the maneuver to prevent his candidacy in the upcoming elections. Lula, Dilma Rousseff, the PT, and the Brazilian people will always have Cuba by their side.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-314-0)[\[311\]](#refnote-314)

### Other repercussions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=34 "Edit section: Other repercussions")\]

- Former President [Fernando Henrique Cardoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Henrique_Cardoso "Fernando Henrique Cardoso") said in an interview with Radio CBN that justice had been served and that it was not a political arrest, given that all the necessary procedures had been followed. However, he stated that he was “not happy.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-315-0)[\[312\]](#refnote-315)
- Former President Dilma Rousseff stated that Lula was innocent and would know how to handle the situation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-316-0)[\[313\]](#refnote-316)
- Former President [José Sarney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Sarney "José Sarney") said he “deeply regretted the arrest” and stated, “I am his friend and was quite shocked.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-317-0)[\[314\]](#refnote-317)
- Businessman and politician [João Doria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Doria "João Doria") said that Brazil woke up relieved and referred to the PT member as a “crook” and a “liar.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-318-0)[\[315\]](#refnote-318)
- The Free Brazil Movement (MBL) and Come to the [Movimento Vem pra Rua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movimento_Vem_pra_Rua "Movimento Vem pra Rua") (MVPR) advocated for Lula's imprisonment, and a month earlier, they called for public demonstrations demanding the former president's arrest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-319-0)[\[316\]](#refnote-319)

In mid-April 2018, an opinion poll conducted by [Datafolha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Datafolha "Datafolha") indicated that 54 percent of voters surveyed by the institute considered Lula's imprisonment to be “fair,” while 40 percent disagreed. Another 62 percent believed that Lula would not run in that year's presidential election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-320-0)[\[317\]](#refnote-320) Shortly after his imprisonment, however, election polls showed that, even though he had been convicted and imprisoned, Lula continued to lead the race for the 2018 election, with about 30 percent of voters saying they would vote for him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-321-0)[\[318\]](#refnote-321) Months later, in August 2018, Lula continued to lead the polls for the 2018 election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-322-0)[\[319\]](#refnote-322)[&#8202;](#ref-back-323-0)[\[320\]](#refnote-323) On the other hand, he also had a high rejection rate of 34 percent, the second highest among the candidates. The polls also showed that, in a scenario without Lula in the election, Jair Bolsonaro led the race.[&#8202;](#ref-back-324-0)[\[321\]](#refnote-324)

Former European leaders, including [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande "François Hollande"), defended Lula's participation in the 2018 elections after his arrest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-325-0)[\[322\]](#refnote-325) The Minister of Foreign Affairs at the time, [Aloysio Nunes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aloysio_Nunes "Aloysio Nunes"), described the demonstration as “arrogant,” saying he was incredulous about the request.[&#8202;](#ref-back-326-0)[\[323\]](#refnote-326)

In December 2019, one month after Lula was released, a new Datafolha poll showed that 54 percent of respondents considered his release to be fair, while 42 percent disagreed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-327-0)[\[324\]](#refnote-327)

In September 2021, former OAS president Léo Pinheiro wrote a letter in which he recanted some of the accusations he had made against Lula in his plea bargain agreement with Operation Car Wash.[&#8202;](#ref-back-328-0)[\[325\]](#refnote-328) The letter was used to dismiss a complaint against Lula, in which he was accused of influence peddling during a lecture in Costa Rica.[&#8202;](#ref-back-329-0)[\[326\]](#refnote-329)

## _Vaza Jato_

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=35 "Edit section: Vaza Jato")\]

Main article: [Vaza Jato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaza_Jato "Vaza Jato")

Starting on 9 June 2019, the online newspaper _The Intercept Brasil_, the Brazilian version of the American publication _The Intercept_, leaked a series of messages from conversations held via the [Telegram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telegram_(software) "Telegram (software)") app between then-judge Sergio Moro, then-prosecutor Deltan Dallagnol, and other members of Operation Car Wash.[&#8202;](#ref-back-330-0)[\[327\]](#refnote-330) The transcripts indicated that Moro provided privileged information to the prosecution, helping the Federal Public Prosecutor's Office (MPF) to build cases, as well as guiding the prosecution, suggesting changes to the phases of Operation Car Wash. They also showed that he demanded speed in new operations, gave strategic advice, provided informal clues, and suggested appeals to the MPF.[&#8202;](#ref-back-331-0)[\[328\]](#refnote-331)

This increased criticism that Moro had not been an impartial judge.[&#8202;](#ref-back-332-0)[\[329\]](#refnote-332) Messages exchanged between prosecutor Deltan Dallagnol and other members of Operation Car Wash indicate that there were doubts about the strength of the evidence presented by the prosecution in the Guarujá triplex case, which led to Lula's imprisonment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-333-0)[\[330\]](#refnote-333)[&#8202;](#ref-back-334-0)[\[331\]](#refnote-334)

## Annulment of convictions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=36 "Edit section: Annulment of convictions")\]

On 8 March 2021, Supreme Court Justice Edson Fachin overturned Lula's convictions on the grounds that they were handed down by a court that did not have jurisdiction to hear the case.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:28-2)[\[6\]](#refnote-:28) The decision granted a habeas corpus petition filed by the former president's defense team in November 2020. Fachin ruled that the Federal Court of Paraná did not have jurisdiction to hear the four cases in the lawsuit, and the cases were transferred to the Federal Court of the Federal District, which must now validate or overturn the decisions of the Federal Court of Paraná.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:28-3)[\[6\]](#refnote-:28) As a result, Lula regained his political rights. Fachin declared null and void all decisions of the 13th Federal Court of Curitiba, which must comply with the decision and refer the case files to the Federal Court of the Federal District, as well as the “loss of the object,” extinguishing fourteen cases pending before the STF questioning Sergio Moro's impartiality in Lula's convictions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-335-0)[\[332\]](#refnote-335) On 15 April 2021, the plenary session of the Federal Supreme Court, by a majority vote from 8 to 3, officially overturned the convictions of the former president handed down by then-judge Sergio Moro in the context of Operation Car Wash.[&#8202;](#ref-back-336-0)[\[333\]](#refnote-336)

## Sergio Moro's suspicion

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=37 "Edit section: Sergio Moro's suspicion")\]

The day after Justice Fachin's decision, the Second Panel of the STF, presided over by Justice Gilmar Mendes, decided to uphold the trial of Moro's suspicion in his actions in the Car Wash investigation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:32-0)[\[334\]](#refnote-:32) In addition to Gilmar Mendes, Justices [Nunes Marques](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nunes_Marques "Nunes Marques"), Cármen Lúcia, and Ricardo Lewandowski voted in favor of upholding the trial.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:32-1)[\[334\]](#refnote-:32) Fachin upheld his decision, arguing that the defense had presented “new and robust evidence, with serious facts,” which would require more time to analyze and that he, as rapporteur of the case, would have the prerogative to prevent the trial from proceeding, due to the loss of the object and prejudice of the habeas corpus petition filed by Lula's defense.[&#8202;](#ref-back-338-0)[\[335\]](#refnote-338) After gaining access to conversations between Moro and Lava Jato prosecutors in Curitiba, the former president's lawyers considered that there were strong arguments demonstrating Moro's bias, such as the disclosure of wiretaps and authorization for coercive conduct.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:32-2)[\[334\]](#refnote-:32)

Most of the ministers in the second panel agreed with several points raised by Lula's defense team. Among the points cited are:[&#8202;](#ref-back-339-0)[\[336\]](#refnote-339)[&#8202;](#ref-back-340-0)[\[337\]](#refnote-340)[&#8202;](#ref-back-341-0)[\[338\]](#refnote-341)[&#8202;](#ref-back-342-0)[\[339\]](#refnote-342)[&#8202;](#ref-back-343-0)[\[340\]](#refnote-343)

- The authorization for Lula's coercive conduct in 2016, without first summoning him to testify and all the “spectacularization” surrounding that coercive conduct;
- The wiretapping of Lula's lawyers without first adopting other investigative measures (the wiretapping of Lula's lawyers was also considered serious by the [Order of Attorneys of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Attorneys_of_Brazil "Order of Attorneys of Brazil"));
- The selective disclosure of Lula's wiretaps, including a conversation with then-President Dilma Rousseff about Lula's move to the [Chief of Staff of the Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Staff_of_the_Presidency "Chief of Staff of the Presidency") in 2016;
- The end of the confidentiality of former Minister [Antônio Palocci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Palocci "Antonio Palocci")'s plea bargain a week before the 2018 election, causing some influence on the election;
- The fact that Sérgio Moro accepted the position of Minister of Justice under Jair Bolsonaro, Lula's political opponent.

On 23 March 2021, the second panel of the Federal Supreme Court granted Habeas Corpus 164.493 filed by Lula's defense and declared former judge Sergio Moro's suspicion in the Guarujá triplex case, which led to the former president's arrest. Justices Gilmar Mendes, Cármen Lúcia, and Ricardo Lewandowski voted in favor of _habeas corpus_, while Justices Edson Fachin and Nunes Marques dissented.[&#8202;](#ref-back-344-0)[\[341\]](#refnote-344) Three months later, the full Supreme Court, by a vote of 7 to 4, upheld the Second Panel's decision declaring Moro suspect.[&#8202;](#ref-back-345-0)[\[342\]](#refnote-345)[&#8202;](#ref-back-346-0)[\[343\]](#refnote-346)

| Favor of suspicion | Against suspicion | Ref |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gilmar Mendes | Edson Fachin | [&#8202;](#ref-back-347-0)[\[344\]](#refnote-347) |
| Alexandre de Moraes | Luís Roberto Barroso |  |
| Ricardo Lewandowski | Marco Aurélio Mello |  |
| Dias Toffoli | Luiz Fux |  |
| Rosa Weber |  |  |
| Cármen Lúcia |  |  |
| Nunes Marques |  |  |

Supreme Court votes on Sergio Moro's recusal in Lula's case

With this decision, the proceedings, which weeks earlier had been redirected by Fachin's decision,[&#8202;](#ref-back-:33-1)[\[21\]](#refnote-:33) also had their preliminary proceedings annulled, so that no action taken by the former judge could be reused by the competent court.[&#8202;](#ref-back-348-0)[\[345\]](#refnote-348)

One day after the STF plenary confirmed the decision of the second panel, Justice Gilmar Mendes extended Moro's recusal to two more cases involving the former president: the Atibaia ranch case and those related to an apartment in São Bernardo do Campo and the Lula Institute.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:34-0)[\[346\]](#refnote-:34) The justice considered that Moro had also acted in these cases. As a result, these cases were also annulled and must start from scratch, so that the actions taken in them cannot be reused by the new judge, as in the case of the triplex.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:34-1)[\[346\]](#refnote-:34)

## Closure

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=38 "Edit section: Closure")\]

With the annulment of Lula's convictions and the suspicion of former judge Sergio Moro by the Federal Supreme Court, the Guarujá triplex case was referred to the Federal Court in Brasilia, where it would be restarted. But on 7 December 2021, the Federal District Attorney's Office requested that the triplex case be dismissed, arguing that it would not be possible to conduct a new investigation and a new trial on the case before it expired.[&#8202;](#ref-back-350-0)[\[347\]](#refnote-350) On 28 January 2022, Judge Pollyanna Alves, of the 12th Federal Criminal Court in Brasília, agreed with the Prosecutor's Office and thus decided to dismiss the triplex case.[&#8202;](#ref-back-351-0)[\[348\]](#refnote-351)

The Federal Prosecutor's Office filed a new complaint against Lula in the Atibaia ranch case, but the complaint was rejected by the judge in Brasília, who considered that the complaint did not provide new evidence to restart the proceedings.[&#8202;](#ref-back-352-0)[\[349\]](#refnote-352) On 27 April 2022, the [United Nations Human Rights Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee "United Nations Human Rights Committee") ruled that Moro had been biased in Lula's trial.[&#8202;](#ref-back-353-0)[\[350\]](#refnote-353)

In the [2022 Brazilian presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_presidential_election "2022 Brazilian presidential election"), Lula was elected president for the third time.[&#8202;](#ref-back-354-0)[\[351\]](#refnote-354)[&#8202;](#ref-back-355-0)[\[352\]](#refnote-355) During the election, one of Lula's prison guards advocated voting for him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-356-0)[\[353\]](#refnote-356) After taking office, Lula nominated lawyer Cristiano Zanin, who defended him in the Operation Car Wash trials, to be a Supreme Court justice, which drew some criticism.[&#8202;](#ref-back-357-0)[\[354\]](#refnote-357)[&#8202;](#ref-back-358-0)[\[355\]](#refnote-358) Lula also chose former Supreme Court Justice Ricardo Lewandowski, who had retired from the Court in 2023, to be Minister of Justice, which drew criticism from former judge Sergio Moro, who said that this would demonstrate that accepting the position of Minister of Justice is not cause for suspicion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-359-0)[\[356\]](#refnote-359) Former prosecutor Deltan Dallagnol also criticized Lewandoswki's nomination and, after being elected federal deputy for Paraná, he was removed from office because, according to Minister [Benedito Gonçalves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benedito_Gon%C3%A7alves "Benedito Gonçalves") of the TSE, he committed “fraud” against the Clean Record Act by requesting resignation from the MPF 11 months before the elections, while facing internal proceedings that could lead to his dismissal — and, consequently, his ineligibility.[&#8202;](#ref-back-360-0)[\[357\]](#refnote-360)[&#8202;](#ref-back-361-0)[\[358\]](#refnote-361)

On 6 September 2023, Supreme Court Justice Dias Toffoli annulled all evidence obtained from Odebrecht's plea bargain statements and stated that Lula's imprisonment was “one of the greatest [judicial errors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miscarriage_of_justice "Miscarriage of justice") in the country's history.”[&#8202;](#ref-back-362-0)[\[359\]](#refnote-362)

## Timeline summary

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=39 "Edit section: Timeline summary")\]

### Guarujá Triplex case

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=40 "Edit section: Guarujá Triplex case")\]

**2016**

- 9 March: Indictment filed by MP-SP (São Paulo State Prosecutor's Office)[&#8202;](#ref-back-olhardireto-0)[\[360\]](#refnote-olhardireto)
- 28 April: The São Paulo court sends the case files to the Federal Court of Paraná[&#8202;](#ref-back-gazeta2016-0)[\[361\]](#refnote-gazeta2016)
- 14 September: MPF (Federal Prosecutor's Office) indicts Lula[&#8202;](#ref-back-opovo2019-0)[\[362\]](#refnote-opovo2019)
- 20 September: [Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro "Sergio Moro") accepts the indictment; Lula becomes a defendant[&#8202;](#ref-back-opovo2019-1)[\[362\]](#refnote-opovo2019)

**2017**

- 10 May: Lula testifies before Moro[&#8202;](#ref-back-opovo2019-2)[\[362\]](#refnote-opovo2019)
- 12 July: Moro convicts Lula (9 years and 6 months)[&#8202;](#ref-back-g1sentenca-0)[\[363\]](#refnote-g1sentenca)

**2018**

- 24 January: TRF-4 upholds Lula's conviction (12 years and 1 month)[&#8202;](#ref-back-folha2018-0)[\[364\]](#refnote-folha2018)
- 6 March: [STJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Court_of_Justice_(Brazil) "Superior Court of Justice (Brazil)") denies habeas corpus by 5–0[&#8202;](#ref-back-stj2018-0)[\[365\]](#refnote-stj2018)
- 22 March: [STF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Federal_Court "Supreme Federal Court") grants Lula a safe conduct until the habeas corpus is judged[&#8202;](#ref-back-opovo2019-3)[\[362\]](#refnote-opovo2019)
- 26 March: TRF-4 rejects Lula's final appeals[&#8202;](#ref-back-opovo2019-4)[\[362\]](#refnote-opovo2019)
- 4 April: STF denies Lula's preventive habeas corpus by 6–5[&#8202;](#ref-back-sul21stf-0)[\[366\]](#refnote-sul21stf)
- 5 April: Moro orders Lula's arrest[&#8202;](#ref-back-uol2018-0)[\[367\]](#refnote-uol2018)
- 7 April: Lula is arrested and taken to the Federal Police[&#8202;](#ref-back-opovo2019-5)[\[362\]](#refnote-opovo2019)
- 8 July: Thompson Flores keeps Lula in prison after the Favreto vs. Gebran Neto dispute[&#8202;](#ref-back-poder360favreto-0)[\[368\]](#refnote-poder360favreto)

**2019**

- 29 January: Death of Lula's brother, Vavá[&#8202;](#ref-back-poder360vava-0)[\[369\]](#refnote-poder360vava)
- 1 March: Death of Lula's grandson, Arthur[&#8202;](#ref-back-moneytimesarthur-0)[\[370\]](#refnote-moneytimesarthur)
- 23 April: STJ upholds Lula's conviction and reduces the sentence to 8 years, 10 months and 20 days[&#8202;](#ref-back-g1stj2019-0)[\[371\]](#refnote-g1stj2019)
- 26 April: First of 22 prison interviews (Folha de S.Paulo and El País)[&#8202;](#ref-back-congressoentrevista-0)[\[372\]](#refnote-congressoentrevista)
- 9 June: Start of the [Vaza Jato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaza_Jato "Vaza Jato") leaks[&#8202;](#ref-back-diariovaza-0)[\[373\]](#refnote-diariovaza)
- 8 November: Lula is released one day after the STF rules that imprisonment before all appeals are exhausted is unconstitutional (580 days in prison – 1 year, 7 months and 1 day)[&#8202;](#ref-back-veja2019-0)[\[374\]](#refnote-veja2019)

**2021**

- 8 March: [Fachin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edson_Fachin "Edson Fachin") annuls Lula's convictions in Lava Jato[&#8202;](#ref-back-g1fachin-0)[\[375\]](#refnote-g1fachin)
- 23 March: STF Second Panel declares Moro biased by 3–2[&#8202;](#ref-back-agazeta2turma-0)[\[376\]](#refnote-agazeta2turma)
- 15 April: STF confirms the annulment of Lula's cases by 8–3[&#8202;](#ref-back-poder360plenary-0)[\[377\]](#refnote-poder360plenary)
- 23 June: STF declares Moro biased by 7–4[&#8202;](#ref-back-stfnews-0)[\[378\]](#refnote-stfnews)
- 7 December: The Federal Prosecutor's Office in the Federal District requests the case be dropped[&#8202;](#ref-back-moneytimesdec2021-0)[\[379\]](#refnote-moneytimesdec2021)

**2022**

- 27 January: Federal Court in Brasília closes the Triplex case[&#8202;](#ref-back-poder360arquivo-0)[\[380\]](#refnote-poder360arquivo)

### Atibaia Ranch case

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Imprisonment_of_Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva&action=edit&section=41 "Edit section: Atibaia Ranch case")\]

**2017**

- 22 May: MPF indicts Lula[&#8202;](#ref-back-diarioatibaia-0)[\[381\]](#refnote-diarioatibaia)
- 1 August: Moro accepts the indictment and Lula becomes a defendant[&#8202;](#ref-back-congressoreu-0)[\[382\]](#refnote-congressoreu)

**2019**

- 6 February: Gabriela Hardt convicts Lula (12 years and 11 months)[&#8202;](#ref-back-poder360stj2021-0)[\[383\]](#refnote-poder360stj2021)
- 27 November: TRF-4 upholds Lula's conviction (17 years, 1 month and 10 days)[&#8202;](#ref-back-poder360trf2019-0)[\[384\]](#refnote-poder360trf2019)

**2020**

- 6 May: TRF-4 rejects appeals and upholds the conviction[&#8202;](#ref-back-poder360trf2020-0)[\[385\]](#refnote-poder360trf2020)

**2021**

- 8 March: Fachin annuls Lula's convictions in Lava Jato[&#8202;](#ref-back-g1fachin-1)[\[375\]](#refnote-g1fachin)

- 24 June: STF extends the ruling on Moro's bias to the ranch case[&#8202;](#ref-back-poder360gilmar-0)[\[386\]](#refnote-poder360gilmar)
- 21 August: Brasília court rejects a new indictment and the case is closed[&#8202;](#ref-back-congressobsb-0)[\[387\]](#refnote-congressobsb)

017. [↑](#ref-back-17-0)["Information note on Human Rights Committee"](https://www.ohchr.org/en/press-releases/2018/08/information-note-human-rights-committee?LangID=E&NewsID=23464). _[United Nations Human Rights Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee "United Nations Human Rights Committee")_. 17 August 2018. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20250124165004/https://www.ohchr.org/en/press-releases/2018/08/information-note-human-rights-committee?LangID=E&NewsID=23464) from the original on 24 January 2025. Retrieved 10 January 2026. The UN Human Rights Committee has requested Brazil to take all necessary measures to ensure that Lula can enjoy and exercise his political rights while in prison, as candidate in the 2018 presidential elections. This includes having appropriate access to the media and members of his political party. The Committee also requested Brazil not to prevent him from standing for election in the 2018 presidential elections, until his appeals before the courts have been completed in fair judicial proceedings. The technical name for this request is "interim measures" and these relate to his pending individual complaint which remains before the Committee. This request does not mean that the Committee has found a violation yet - it is an urgent measure to preserve Lula's right, pending the case consideration on the merits, which will take place next year. It is important to note that although this response is being provided through the UN Human Rights Office, it is a decision of the Human Rights Committee, which is made up of independent experts. This response may be attributed to the Human Rights Committee.

A ação é limitada, e foi tomada para garantir que os direitos do sr. Lula da Silva não sejam minados de forma irreparável enquanto sua queixa está pendente diante do Comitê.\[Brazil has therefore made a legal commitment to listen to the Human Rights Committee. The Committee's actions are based directly on the legal obligations that Brazil assumed by becoming a party to the Covenant and the Optional Protocol. The action is limited and was taken to ensure that Mr. Lula da Silva's rights are not irreparably undermined while his complaint is pending before the Committee.\]

025. [Jump up to: 1](#ref-back-:22-0) [2](#ref-back-:22-1)["Views adopted by the Committee under article 5 (4) of the Optional Protocol, concerning communication No. 2841/2016"](https://static.poder360.com.br/2022/04/ONU-Moro.pdf)(PDF). _[United Nations Human Rights Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee "United Nations Human Rights Committee")_. 27 March 2022. In the author's case, the Committee observes that it has already concluded that the criminal proceedings against him and his subsequent conviction violated due process guarantees provided for in article 14 of the Covenant. Therefore, the Committee finds that the consequent ban on the author's right to run for elections as well as the restriction on his right to vote constituted a violation of article 25 (b). \[...\] the Committee wishes to receive from the State party, within 180 days, information about the measures taken to give effect to the Committee's Views. The State party is also requested to publish the present Views, have them translated into the official language of the State party and to disseminate them widely.
